# 内部考试体系-大纲 (Internal Exam System - Syllabus)

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-img_v3_02ui_0cf83716-94de-45d8-afaf-ae81a71cddag.jpg>)

**注：**入门考试，只考1星内容。期权、美国国债只考概念，美国国债在入门资料中不作为独立章节出现。
**Note:** The entry-level exam only covers 1-star content. Options and U.S. Treasuries are only tested on concepts; U.S. Treasuries do not appear as an independent chapter in the entry-level materials.

# 1. 宏观经济学（Macroeconomics）

> **考试小贴士 (Exam Tips)**：经济学科目题目占比为18%-20%左右。
> The Economics subject accounts for about 18%-20% of the questions.
>
> *   1.1的全部内容为重点考察对象，需要员工理解经济周期的形成以及不同阶段的特征。
>     All content in 1.1 is a key examination target; employees need to understand the formation of business cycles and the characteristics of different stages.
> *   1.2全部为重点考察对象，需了解货币政策是什么、有哪些目标以及美联储有哪些货币政策工具。
>     All content in 1.2 is a key examination target; need to understand what monetary policy is, its goals, and the tools available to the Fed.
> *   1.3只需了解财政政策的基本信息即可，考试题目更多停留在概念。
>     For 1.3, only basic information on fiscal policy is needed; exam questions are mostly conceptual.
> *   1.4全部为重点考察内容，需要重点理解如何运用货币政策/财政政策对抗经济周期以及不同资产（股票/债券）在不同经济环境下的表现。
>     All content in 1.4 is key; focus on understanding how to use monetary/fiscal policy to counter business cycles and the performance of different assets (stocks/bonds) in different economic environments.
> *   1.5案例分析主要是帮助大家理解股/债在不同环境下的表现，考试会考少部分基于案例的情景分析。
>     1.5 Case studies mainly help understand stock/bond performance in different environments; the exam will include a few scenario analyses based on cases.
> *   **产品层面**：掌握MooMoo App内常见的经济指标，并知晓具体查找路径。
>     **Product Level**: Master common economic indicators in the MooMoo App and know their specific search paths.

## 1.1 经济周期与常见经济指标（Business Cycle & Common Economic Indicator Learning Material）

### 1.1.1 经济周期基础知识（Fundamentals of the Business Cycle）

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image1.png>)

**English:**
**The business cycle refers to the recurring, irregular fluctuations in aggregate economic activity around the economy’s long-term potential growth trend.** It is officially determined by the U.S. National Bureau of Economic Research (NBER), which defines a "recession" as a significant decline in economic activity spread across the economy, lasting more than a few months—encompassing production, employment, and income. The business cycle’s duration varies (typically 2–10 years) and is driven by shifts in aggregate demand and aggregate supply.

**中文:**
**经济周期是指总体经济活动围绕经济长期潜在增长趋势发生的周期性、不规则波动。** 它由美国国家经济研究局 (NBER) 正式确定，NBER 将“衰退”定义为经济活动在整个经济中显著下降，持续时间超过几个月——包括生产、就业和收入。经济周期的持续时间各不相同（通常为 2-10 年），并受总需求和总供给变化的驱动。

### 1.1.2 经济周期的四个阶段（Explanation of Four Phases of Business Cycle）

#### 1. 扩张期 (Expansion)
*   **Core Economic Characteristics**: Economic activity rises above potential output; real GDP grows steadily; employment expands; business investment increases; consumer confidence strengthens.
    *   **核心经济特征**: 经济活动高于潜在产出；实际 GDP 稳步增长；就业扩大；商业投资增加；消费者信心增强。
*   **Key Indicator Trends**: Real GDP growth: 2%–3% (U.S. long-term trend); Unemployment rate: Below natural rate (4%–4.5%); Core inflation: 1%–2% (near Fed target).
    *   **关键指标趋势**: 实际 GDP 增长：2%–3%（美国长期趋势）；失业率：低于自然失业率（4%–4.5%）；核心通胀：1%–2%（接近美联储目标）。
*   **U.S. Market Relevance**: 2009–2020 U.S. expansion.
    *   **美国市场相关性**: 2009–2020 年美国经济扩张。

#### 2. 顶峰期 (Peak)
*   **Core Economic Characteristics**: Economic growth slows to a halt; output reaches maximum level; inflationary pressures peak as capacity constraints emerge.
    *   **核心经济特征**: 经济增长放缓至停滞；产出达到最高水平；随着产能限制的出现，通胀压力达到顶峰。
*   **Key Indicator Trends**: Real GDP growth: <1%; Unemployment rate: Trough (often <4%); Core inflation: >2.5% (above target).
    *   **关键指标趋势**: 实际 GDP 增长：<1%；失业率：低谷（通常 <4%）；核心通胀：>2.5%（高于目标）。
*   **U.S. Market Relevance**: February 2020 U.S. peak.
    *   **美国市场相关性**: 2020 年 2 月美国经济顶峰。

#### 3. 收缩期/衰退期 (Contraction / Recession)
*   **Core Economic Characteristics**: Economic activity falls below potential output; real GDP declines; businesses cut investment; layoffs increase; consumer spending weakens.
    *   **核心经济特征**: 经济活动低于潜在产出；实际 GDP 下降；企业削减投资；裁员增加；消费支出减弱。
*   **Key Indicator Trends**: Real GDP growth: Negative for ≥2 quarters; Unemployment rate: Rises ≥1 percentage point; Core inflation: Decelerates.
    *   **关键指标趋势**: 实际 GDP 增长：连续 ≥2 个季度为负；失业率：上升 ≥1 个百分点；核心通胀：减速。
*   **U.S. Market Relevance**: 2007–2009 Global Financial Crisis recession, U.S. unemployment rose from 4.7% to 10%.
    *   **美国市场相关性**: 2007–2009 年全球金融危机衰退，美国失业率从 4.7% 上升至 10%。

#### 4. 谷底期 (Trough)
*   **Core Economic Characteristics**: Economic decline stops; output hits bottom; pessimism fades; businesses and consumers begin to rebuild confidence.
    *   **核心经济特征**: 经济下滑停止；产出触底；悲观情绪消退；企业和消费者开始重建信心。
*   **Key Indicator Trends**: Real GDP growth: Stabilizes near 0%; Unemployment rate: Peaks; Core inflation: May reach a floor.
    *   **关键指标趋势**: 实际 GDP 增长：稳定在 0% 附近；失业率：见顶；核心通胀：可能触底。
*   **U.S. Market Relevance**: April 2020 U.S. trough.
    *   **美国市场相关性**: 2020 年 4 月美国经济谷底。

### 1.1.3 经济周期的关键驱动因素（Key Drivers of the U.S. Business Cycle）

**Factors directly influencing U.S. business cycle fluctuations / 直接影响美国经济周期波动的因素**

*   **Consumer Spending (消费支出)**:
    *   **English**: Consumer spending accounts for approximately 68%–70% of U.S. GDP, making it the primary driver of aggregate demand. Changes in consumer confidence (tied to employment security and wage growth, per textbook) directly impact retail sales, housing demand, and discretionary spending (e.g., autos, electronics)—critical for U.S. equity sectors like consumer staples and discretionary.
    *   **中文**: 消费支出约占美国 GDP 的 68%–70%，是总需求的主要驱动力。消费者信心的变化（与就业保障和工资增长相关）直接影响零售销售、住房需求和非必需消费支出（如汽车、电子产品）——这对于必需消费品和非必需消费品等美国股票板块至关重要。

*   **Business Investment (商业投资)**:
    *   **English**: Includes spending on machinery, equipment, and commercial real estate. U.S. Business investment is sensitive to interest rates (Fed policy), corporate profit margins, and technological innovation (e.g., AI-related investment boom in 2023–2024).
    *   **中文**: 包括在机械、设备和商业房地产上的支出。美国商业投资对利率（美联储政策）、企业利润率和技术创新（例如 2023-2024 年 AI 相关的投资热潮）非常敏感。

*   **Monetary Policy (Federal Reserve) (货币政策 - 美联储)**:
    *   **English**: Fed’s adjustments to the federal funds rate and balance sheet (quantitative easing/QE, quantitative tightening/QT) directly influence borrowing costs for businesses and consumers, asset prices (stocks, bonds), and inflation.
    *   **中文**: 美联储对联邦基金利率和资产负债表（量化宽松/QE、量化紧缩/QT）的调整直接影响企业和消费者的借贷成本、资产价格（股票、债券）以及通胀。

*   **Net Exports (净出口)**:
    *   **English**: Tied to the U.S. dollar exchange rate and global demand. A stronger USD (e.g., 2022) reduces U.S. export competitiveness, while weak global demand (e.g., 2023 Eurozone slowdown) weighs on U.S. export growth.
    *   **中文**: 与美元汇率和全球需求相关。美元走强（如 2022 年）会降低美国出口竞争力，而全球需求疲软（如 2023 年欧元区放缓）则会拖累美国出口增长。

### 1.1.4 美国常见经济指标（Summary of Common U.S. Economic Indicators & Explanations）

| Indicator Category (指标类别) | Specific Indicators (具体指标) | Basis for Classification (分类依据) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Leading Indicators (先行指标)**<br>(Predict future cycle turning points / 预测未来周期转折点) | ISM Manufacturing PMI, Core Durable Goods Orders, Consumer Confidence Index (CCI), 10-Year Treasury Yield & 10Y-2Y Spread | Change ahead of economic cycle; reflect future spending/investment plans or predictive relationships (e.g., yield curve inversion precedes recessions).<br>在经济周期之前变化；反映未来的支出/投资计划或预测关系（例如，收益率曲线倒挂先于衰退）。 |
| **Coincident Indicators (同步指标)**<br>(Change with the economic cycle / 随经济周期变化) | Gross Domestic Product (GDP), Nonfarm Payrolls (NFP), Retail Sales | Directly reflect current economic activity; move in tandem with aggregate output and employment trends.<br>直接反映当前经济活动；与总产出和就业趋势同步变动。 |
| **Lagging Indicators (滞后指标)**<br>(Confirm cycle turning points after they occur / 确认已发生的周期转折点) | Unemployment Rate, CPI, PCE Price Index | Adjust after economic activity changes; used to confirm cycle trends (e.g., unemployment peaks post-trough).<br>在经济活动变化后调整；用于确认周期趋势（例如，失业率在谷底后见顶）。 |

**Detailed Indicators (详细指标解析)**

These indicators are closely monitored by U.S. financial markets to gauge business cycle stages, inflation, and policy trends. Each includes a market-relevant explanation:
这些指标受到美国金融市场的密切监控，用于衡量经济周期阶段、通胀和政策趋势。每个指标都包含与市场相关的解释：

#### 1. Gross Domestic Product (GDP) [Coincident Indicator]
*   **Definition**: The total market value of all final goods and services produced within a country’s borders in a given period. The BEA releases quarterly data with three revisions; real GDP (adjusted for inflation) is the key metric for cycle analysis (nominal GDP is less useful for trend identification).
    *   **定义**: 一个国家在一定时期内生产的所有最终商品和服务的总市场价值。BEA 每季度发布数据，并进行三次修订；实际 GDP（经通胀调整）是周期分析的关键指标（名义 GDP 对趋势识别用处不大）。
*   **Explanation**: The broadest measure of economic activity. Real GDP growth above 2% (U.S. potential growth rate) indicates expansion; growth below 1% signals slowing activity; negative growth for ≥2 quarters is a common recession proxy (supplementary to NBER’s definition). Market reactions: strong GDP may raise Fed hike expectations (pressuring bonds); weak growth may signal easing (bonds outperform).
    *   **解释**: 最广泛的经济活动衡量指标。实际 GDP 增长超过 2%（美国潜在增长率）表明扩张；增长低于 1% 表明活动放缓；连续 ≥2 个季度负增长是常见的衰退指标（作为 NBER 定义的补充）。市场反应：强劲的 GDP 可能提高美联储加息预期（施压债券）；增长疲软可能预示宽松（债券表现优异）。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image2.png>)

#### 2. Nonfarm Payrolls (NFP) [Coincident Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly BLS data measuring the change in employment across all sectors except agriculture, private households, and non-profits—considered the most reliable gauge of U.S. labor market trends.
    *   **定义**: BLS 每月发布的数据，衡量除农业、私人家庭和非营利组织以外的所有行业的就业变化——被认为是衡量美国劳动力市场趋势最可靠的指标。
*   **Explanation**: NFP is the most impactful monthly indicator. Average monthly gains of 150k–200k signal stable employment; gains >300k indicate tight labor markets; gains <100k suggest slowing growth. The changes in NFP directly links to consumer spending (68%–70% of GDP) and Fed policy.
    *   **解释**: NFP 是最具影响力的月度指标。月均增长 15 万-20 万表明就业稳定；增长 >30 万表明劳动力市场紧张；增长 <10 万表明增长放缓。NFP 的变化直接联系到消费支出（占 GDP 的 68%-70%）和美联储政策。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image3.png>)

#### 3. Unemployment Rate [Lagging Indicator]
*   **Definition**: BLS monthly data measuring the percentage of the labor force (employed + actively seeking work) that is unemployed; excludes discouraged workers (a key limitation noted in the textbook).
    *   **定义**: BLS 每月发布的数据，衡量劳动力（就业 + 积极寻找工作）中失业的百分比；不包括丧失信心的工人（教科书中指出的一个关键局限性）。
*   **Explanation**: The U.S. natural rate of unemployment (full employment) is 4%–4.5% Unemployment below this range creates wage inflation pressures; above 6% (e.g., 14.7% in 2020) signals recessionary conditions.
    *   **解释**: 美国的自然失业率（充分就业）为 4%-4.5%。失业率低于此范围会产生工资通胀压力；高于 6%（例如 2020 年的 14.7%）则预示衰退状况。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image4.png>)

#### 4. Consumer Price Index (CPI) [Lagging Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly BLS data measuring the change in prices of a fixed basket of goods/services (food, energy, housing, healthcare) purchased by urban consumers. Core CPI (excludes food/energy) is preferred for trend analysis due to volatility (textbook clarification).
    *   **定义**: BLS 每月发布的数据，衡量城市消费者购买的一篮子固定商品/服务（食品、能源、住房、医疗保健）的价格变化。由于波动性，核心 CPI（不包括食品/能源）更适合趋势分析。
*   **Explanation**: The primary market-watched inflation gauge. Core CPI growth above 3% triggers Fed hawkishness; below 1% raises deflation risks. Critical for bonds (inflation erodes fixed returns) and stocks (high inflation compresses margins).
    *   **解释**: 市场主要关注的通胀指标。核心 CPI 增长超过 3% 会引发美联储鹰派立场；低于 1% 则增加通缩风险。对债券（通胀侵蚀固定回报）和股票（高通胀压缩利润率）至关重要。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image5.png>)

#### 5. Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) Price Index [Lagging Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly BEA data measuring prices of goods/services purchased by consumers, with a more flexible basket than CPI (e.g., includes employer-paid healthcare). Core PCE (excludes food/energy) is the Fed’s preferred inflation gauge.
    *   **定义**: BEA 每月发布的数据，衡量消费者购买的商品/服务的价格，其篮子比 CPI 更灵活（例如，包括雇主支付的医疗保健）。核心 PCE（不包括食品/能源）是美联储首选的通胀指标。
*   **Explanation**: Directly guides Fed policy (2% target). Core PCE above 2% sustains hawkishness; below 2% supports dovishness. PCE is less volatile than CPI, leading to more muted but longer-lasting market reactions.
    *   **解释**: 直接指导美联储政策（2% 目标）。核心 PCE 高于 2% 维持鹰派；低于 2% 支持鸽派。PCE 比 CPI 波动小，导致市场反应较温和但更持久。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image6.png>)

#### 6. ISM Manufacturing PMI [Leading Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly ISM survey of manufacturing firms, measuring new orders, production, employment, supplier deliveries, and inventories. A reading >50 indicates expansion; <50 indicates contraction.
    *   **定义**: ISM 每月对制造业公司进行的调查，衡量新订单、生产、就业、供应商交货和库存。读数 >50 表示扩张；<50 表示收缩。
*   **Explanation**: A leading indicator (PMI >55 signals strong industrial growth; <45 suggests manufacturing recession. The new orders sub-index is a key predictor of future GDP growth.
    *   **解释**: 先行指标（PMI >55 表明工业增长强劲；<45 表明制造业衰退。新订单子指数是未来 GDP 增长的关键预测指标）。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——财经日历

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image7.PNG>)

#### 7. Retail Sales [Coincident Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly BLS data measuring total sales of retail establishments (including food services and autos). Core retail sales (excludes autos/gasoline) is a better proxy for consumer spending trends.
    *   **定义**: BLS 每月发布的数据，衡量零售机构（包括食品服务和汽车）的总销售额。核心零售销售（不包括汽车/汽油）更能代表消费支出趋势。
*   **Explanation**: Reflects consumer health (68%–70% of GDP). Monthly growth >0.5% indicates strong demand (supports consumer stocks); negative growth signals weak confidence. Critical for assessing expansion sustainability.
    *   **解释**: 反映消费者健康状况（占 GDP 的 68%-70%）。月度增长 >0.5% 表明需求强劲（支持消费股）；负增长表明信心疲软。对评估扩张的可持续性至关重要。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image8.png>)

#### 8. Durable Goods Orders [Leading Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly BEA data measuring new orders for goods with a useful life >3 years (autos, machinery, aircraft). Core orders (excludes transportation) are a proxy for business investment (reduces volatility).
    *   **定义**: BEA 每月发布的数据，衡量使用寿命 >3 年的商品（汽车、机械、飞机）的新订单。核心订单（不包括运输）是商业投资的代表（减少波动）。
*   **Explanation**: A leading indicator of investment and industrial activity. Strong growth suggests business expansion (benefits industrials/tech); declining numbers signal weak confidence (recession risk).
    *   **解释**: 投资和工业活动的先行指标。强劲增长表明业务扩张（利好工业/科技）；数字下降表明信心疲软（衰退风险）。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——精选宏观数据

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image9.png>)

#### 9. Consumer Confidence Index (CCI) [Leading Indicator]
*   **Definition**: Monthly Conference Board survey measuring consumers’ assessment of current conditions and future expectations (0–100 scale).
    *   **定义**: 世界大型企业联合会每月的调查，衡量消费者对当前状况和未来预期的评估（0-100 刻度）。
*   **Explanation**: High CCI (>100, e.g., 102.6 in 2023) signals strong spending intent (supports retail stocks); low CCI (<80, e.g., current reading of 54) indicates caution. The expectations sub-index is a leading indicator.
    *   **解释**: 高 CCI（>100，例如 2023 年的 102.6）表明强烈的消费意愿（支持零售股）；低 CCI（<80，例如当前的 54）表明谨慎。预期子指数是先行指标。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——美股——财经日历

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image10.PNG>)

#### 10. 10-Year Treasury Yield & Yield Curve (10Y-2Y Spread) [Leading Indicator]
*   **Definition**: The 10-year Treasury yield is the benchmark for long-term borrowing costs; the 10Y-2Y spread is the difference between 10-year and 2-year yields—used to predict cycle turning points.
    *   **定义**: 10 年期国债收益率是长期借贷成本的基准；10 年期-2 年期利差是 10 年期和 2 年期收益率之间的差异——用于预测周期转折点。
*   **Explanation**: An inverted spread (10Y < 2Y) has preceded every U.S. recession since 1950 (6–18-month lag). A steepening spread signals expansion expectation. The 10-year yield benchmarks mortgages/corporate bonds.
    *   **解释**: 自 1950 年以来，利差倒挂（10Y < 2Y）先于每次美国经济衰退出现（滞后 6-18 个月）。利差陡峭化表明扩张预期。10 年期收益率是抵押贷款/公司债券的基准。
*   **MooMoo App**: 市场——更多——债券

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image11.PNG>)

## 1.2 货币政策（Monetary Policy/U.S. Fiscal Policy）

> **Note**: This chapter is designed for firm employees to understand the Federal Reserve's (Fed) interest rate policies, **specifically rate hikes and cuts.** It also covers the objectives and implementation of the U.S. fiscal policy.
> **说明**: 本章旨在帮助公司员工了解美联储 (Fed) 的利率政策，**特别是加息和降息。** 它还涵盖了美国财政政策的目标和实施。

### 1.2.1 美联储货币政策概述（Federal Reserve Monetary Policy Overview）

**English:**
The Federal Reserve, through the Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC), sets the target range for the federal funds rate—the interest rate at which banks lend reserves to each other overnight. This rate influences broader borrowing costs across the economy, including mortgages, corporate loans, and consumer credit. The **Fed's dual mandate is to promote maximum employment and stable prices (targeting 2% inflation).**

Historical fed funds rate cycles since 1980 include multiple hikes and cuts, often in response to economic conditions. For instance:
*   **Hike Cycles**: Examples include 1994-1995 (from 3% to 6%), 1999-2000 (4.75% to 6.5%), 2004-2006 (1% to 5.25%), 2015-2018 (0.25% to 2.5%), and 2022-2023 (0.25% to 5.5%).
*   **Cut Cycles**: Notable periods include 2001 (6.5% to 1.75%), 2007-2008 (5.25% to 0-0.25%), 2019 (2.5% to 1.75%), and 2024-2025 (5.5% to approximately 4-4.25% by late 2025).

These cycles reflect the Fed's reactive approach: tightening during expansions to curb inflation and easing during slowdowns to stimulate growth.

**中文:**
美联储通过联邦公开市场委员会 (FOMC) 设定联邦基金利率的目标范围——即银行之间隔夜拆借准备金的利率。该利率影响整个经济中更广泛的借贷成本，包括抵押贷款、企业贷款和消费信贷。**美联储的双重使命是促进最大化就业和价格稳定（目标是 2% 的通胀率）。**

自 1980 年以来的历史联邦基金利率周期包括多次加息和降息，通常是对经济状况的反应。例如：
*   **加息周期**: 例子包括 1994-1995 年（从 3% 到 6%）、1999-2000 年（4.75% 到 6.5%）、2004-2006 年（1% 到 5.25%）、2015-2018 年（0.25% 到 2.5%）以及 2022-2023 年（0.25% 到 5.5%）。
*   **降息周期**: 显著时期包括 2001 年（6.5% 到 1.75%）、2007-2008 年（5.25% 到 0-0.25%）、2019 年（2.5% 到 1.75%）以及 2024-2025 年（5.5% 到 2025 年底约 4-4.25%）。

这些周期反映了美联储的反应式方法：在扩张期间紧缩以抑制通胀，在经济放缓期间宽松以刺激增长。

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image12.png>)

*Graph: Unemployment rate, Core CPI and Federal funds rate / 图表：失业率、核心 CPI 和联邦基金利率*

### 1.2.2 主要货币工具（Key Monetary Policy Tools）

#### 1. 公开市场操作 (Open Market Operations, OMO)
*   **English**: OMO is one of the Fed's oldest and most traditional tools. It involves the buying and selling of government securities (primarily U.S. Treasury bonds and mortgage-backed securities) in the open market to adjust the level of reserves in the banking system.
    *   **How it works**: When the Fed buys securities (expansionary OMO), it injects money into the economy by crediting banks' reserve accounts, increasing the money supply and lowering interest rates. Conversely, selling securities (contractionary OMO) withdraws reserves, reducing the money supply and raising rates.
    *   **Role in policy**: OMO helps the Fed achieve its target federal funds rate. During quantitative easing (QE) programs, like those in 2008–2014 and 2020–2022, the Fed used large-scale asset purchases to lower long-term interest rates and support economic recovery. In normal times, OMO fine-tunes short-term rates.
    *   **Example**: In response to the COVID-19 pandemic, the Fed expanded its balance sheet through OMO to provide liquidity and stabilize markets.

*   **中文**: OMO 是美联储最古老和最传统的工具之一。它涉及在公开市场上买卖政府证券（主要是美国国债和抵押贷款支持证券），以调整银行体系中的准备金水平。
    *   **运作方式**: 当美联储购买证券（扩张性 OMO）时，它通过贷记银行的准备金账户向经济注入资金，从而增加货币供应量并降低利率。相反，出售证券（紧缩性 OMO）会回笼准备金，减少货币供应量并提高利率。
    *   **政策作用**: OMO 帮助美联储实现其联邦基金利率目标。在量化宽松 (QE) 计划期间（如 2008-2014 年和 2020-2022 年），美联储通过大规模资产购买来降低长期利率并支持经济复苏。在正常时期，OMO 用于微调短期利率。
    *   **示例**: 为应对 COVID-19 大流行，美联储通过 OMO 扩大其资产负债表，以提供流动性并稳定市场。

#### 2. 准备金余额利息 (Interest On Reserve Balances, IORB)
*   **English**: Introduced in 2008, IORB is the interest rate the Fed pays on reserves that banks hold at Federal Reserve Banks. This includes both required reserves (a small fraction of deposits) and excess reserves.
    *   **How it works**: By setting the IORB rate, the Fed establishes a "floor" for short-term interest rates. Banks are unlikely to lend reserves below this rate since they can earn IORB risk-free. Raising IORB tightens policy by encouraging banks to hold reserves rather than lend, while lowering it encourages lending.
    *   **Role in policy**: In the ample-reserves framework (post-2008), IORB is the primary tool for controlling the federal funds rate. It replaced reliance on scarce reserves and direct OMO for day-to-day adjustments. As of 2026, the IORB rate is adjusted during FOMC meetings to align with economic conditions.
    *   **Example**: If inflation rises, the Fed might hike IORB to 5% from 4.5%, pulling up market rates and slowing economic activity.

*   **中文**: IORB 于 2008 年推出，是美联储对银行在联邦储备银行持有的准备金支付的利率。这包括法定准备金（存款的一小部分）和超额准备金。
    *   **运作方式**: 通过设定 IORB 利率，美联储为短期利率设立了“下限”。银行不太可能以低于此利率的利率放贷，因为它们可以无风险地赚取 IORB。提高 IORB 会鼓励银行持有准备金而不是放贷，从而紧缩政策；而降低 IORB 则鼓励放贷。
    *   **政策作用**: 在充足准备金框架下（2008 年后），IORB 是控制联邦基金利率的主要工具。它取代了对稀缺准备金和直接 OMO 进行日常调整的依赖。截至 2026 年，IORB 利率在 FOMC 会议期间根据经济状况进行调整。
    *   **示例**: 如果通胀上升，美联储可能会将 IORB 从 4.5% 提高到 5%，从而推高市场利率并减缓经济活动。

#### 3. 隔夜逆回购协议 (Overnight Reverse Repurchase Agreements, ONRRP)
*   **English**: The ONRRP facility allows eligible counterparties (like money market funds and government-sponsored enterprises) to lend cash to the Fed overnight in exchange for Treasury securities as collateral. It's essentially a way for the Fed to borrow short-term funds.
    *   **How it works**: The Fed sets an offering rate for ONRRP, which acts as a supplementary floor for short-term rates. Participants deposit funds at this rate, reducing excess liquidity in the market. Increasing the ONRRP rate absorbs more liquidity, while decreasing it releases it.
    *   **Role in policy**: ONRRP complements IORB by providing a floor for non-bank institutions that can't earn IORB directly. It's crucial in managing abundant reserves and preventing rates from falling too low. Usage surged in 2021–2023 as liquidity flooded the system from QE.
    *   **Example**: During periods of high liquidity (e.g., post-stimulus), the Fed raises the ONRRP rate to 0.05% above the target floor, ensuring money market rates stay aligned with policy goals.

*   **中文**: ONRRP 机制允许合格的交易对手（如货币市场基金和政府资助企业）将现金隔夜借给美联储，以换取国债作为抵押品。这本质上是美联储借入短期资金的一种方式。
    *   **运作方式**: 美联储设定 ONRRP 的发行利率，作为短期利率的补充下限。参与者按此利率存入资金，从而减少市场上的过剩流动性。提高 ONRRP 利率会吸收更多流动性，而降低则会释放流动性。
    *   **政策作用**: ONRRP 通过为不能直接赚取 IORB 的非银行机构提供下限来补充 IORB。这对于管理充裕的准备金和防止利率跌得太低至关重要。随着 QE 带来的流动性泛滥，其使用量在 2021-2023 年激增。
    *   **示例**: 在高流动性时期（例如刺激计划后），美联储将 ONRRP 利率提高到目标下限以上 0.05%，以确保货币市场利率与政策目标保持一致。

#### 4. 贴现率 (Discount Rate)
*   **English**: The discount rate is the interest rate charged to commercial banks and other depository institutions for short-term loans from the Fed's discount window. There are three tiers: primary credit (for healthy banks), secondary credit (for troubled ones), and seasonal credit.
    *   **How it works**: Banks borrow from the discount window as a last resort for liquidity needs. The primary discount rate is typically set 0.5–1% above the federal funds target, acting as a ceiling for rates. Lowering it signals easier credit, while raising it tightens conditions.
    *   **Role in policy**: It's a backup tool for emergency liquidity, reducing stigma through reforms post-2008. It supports financial stability rather than day-to-day rate control but influences overall borrowing costs.
    *   **Example**: In crises like 2008 or 2020, the Fed cut the discount rate to near zero to encourage borrowing and prevent bank runs.

*   **中文**: 贴现率是美联储向商业银行和其他存款机构提供短期贷款（通过贴现窗口）收取的利率。分为三级：一级信贷（针对健康银行）、二级信贷（针对问题银行）和季节性信贷。
    *   **运作方式**: 银行通过贴现窗口借款作为满足流动性需求的最后手段。一级贴现率通常设定在联邦基金目标之上 0.5-1%，作为利率的上限。降低它意味着信贷宽松，而提高它则意味着紧缩。
    *   **政策作用**: 它是紧急流动性的备用工具，通过 2008 年后的改革减少了耻辱感。它支持金融稳定而不是日常利率控制，但会影响整体借贷成本。
    *   **示例**: 在 2008 年或 2020 年等危机中，美联储将贴现率降至接近零，以鼓励借贷并防止银行挤兑。

### 1.2.3 政策实施中这些工具的相互作用 (How These Tools Interact in Policy Implementation)

**English:**
The Fed uses these tools in concert within its ample-reserves regime:
*   **Setting the target range**: The FOMC announces a target range for the federal funds rate (e.g., 5.25–5.50%).
*   **Floor system**: IORB and ONRRP keep rates from falling below the target by providing attractive returns on reserves and repo transactions.
*   **Ceiling and adjustments**: The discount rate caps rates from rising too high, while OMO adds or removes reserves as needed for fine-tuning.
*   **Quantitative tools**: In extraordinary times, large-scale OMO (QE or quantitative tightening) expands or shrinks the balance sheet to influence longer-term rates and asset prices.

**中文:**
美联储在充足准备金制度下协同使用这些工具：
*   **设定目标范围**: FOMC 宣布联邦基金利率的目标范围（例如 5.25–5.50%）。
*   **下限系统**: IORB 和 ONRRP 通过为准备金和回购交易提供有吸引力的回报，防止利率跌破目标。
*   **上限和调整**: 贴现率限制利率升得太高，而 OMO 根据需要增加或移除准备金以进行微调。
*   **量化工具**: 在特殊时期，大规模 OMO（QE 或量化紧缩）扩大或缩小资产负债表，以影响长期利率和资产价格。

| Tool (工具) | Primary Function (主要功能) | Expansionary Use (扩张性用途) | Contractionary Use (紧缩性用途) | Key Interaction (关键相互作用) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OMO** | Adjust reserve levels<br>调整准备金水平 | Buy securities (inject liquidity)<br>购买证券（注入流动性） | Sell securities (withdraw liquidity)<br>出售证券（回笼流动性） | Complements IORB/ONRRP for balance sheet management<br>补充 IORB/ONRRP 进行资产负债表管理 |
| **IORB** | Set floor for rates<br>设定利率下限 | Lower rate (encourage lending)<br>降低利率（鼓励放贷） | Raise rate (encourage holding reserves)<br>提高利率（鼓励持有准备金） | Main tool for daily rate control<br>日常利率控制的主要工具 |
| **ONRRP** | Absorb excess liquidity<br>吸收过剩流动性 | Lower rate (release funds)<br>降低利率（释放资金） | Raise rate (absorb funds)<br>提高利率（吸收资金） | Supports IORB for non-banks<br>支持非银行机构的 IORB |
| **Discount Rate** | Provide emergency liquidity<br>提供紧急流动性 | Lower rate (cheaper borrowing)<br>降低利率（借贷更便宜） | Raise rate (discourage borrowing)<br>提高利率（抑制借贷） | Acts as a safety net ceiling<br>充当安全网上限 |

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image13.png>)

*Graph: IORB/ONRRP work as the floor for rates, discount rate works as the ceiling / 图表：IORB/ONRRP 作为利率下限，贴现率作为上限*

## 1.3 美国财政政策（Introduction to U.S. Fiscal Policy）

### 1.3.1 基础概念（Policy Logic and Key Concepts）

**English:**
**Fiscal policy** refers to the decisions made by the U.S. government regarding taxation and spending to influence the overall economy. It is managed primarily by Congress and the President, with the goal of achieving macroeconomic stability, sustainable growth, full employment, and price stability (i.e., controlling inflation). Unlike monetary policy, which is handled by the Federal Reserve through interest rates and money supply, fiscal policy uses the federal budget as its main tool. The underlying logic is that government actions can smooth out economic fluctuations, support long-term prosperity, and address inequalities.

**中文:**
**财政政策**是指美国政府关于税收和支出以影响整体经济的决策。它主要由国会和总统管理，目标是实现宏观经济稳定、可持续增长、充分就业和价格稳定（即控制通胀）。与由美联储通过利率和货币供应量管理的货币政策不同，财政政策使用联邦预算作为其主要工具。其基本逻辑是政府行动可以平滑经济波动，支持长期繁荣并解决不平等问题。

### 1.3.2 核心目标（Core Objectives of U.S. Fiscal Policy）

**English:**
The policy logic is rooted in Keynesian economics, which argues that governments should actively intervene to manage demand in the economy. **The main goals include:**
*   **Economic Growth**: Stimulating activity during slowdowns.
*   **Full Employment**: Reducing unemployment by creating jobs.
*   **Price Stability**: Preventing high inflation or deflation.
*   **Income Redistribution**: Using taxes and spending to reduce inequality.

These objectives are pursued through **two primary tools:**
*   **Taxation**: Adjusting tax rates (e.g., income, corporate, or sales taxes) to influence how much money people and businesses have to spend.
*   **Government Spending**: Allocating funds for programs like infrastructure, education, defense, or social welfare.

The U.S. Treasury Department implements these, often through annual budgets proposed by the President and approved by Congress.

**中文:**
该政策逻辑植根于凯恩斯主义经济学，认为政府应积极干预以管理经济需求。**主要目标包括：**
*   **经济增长**: 在经济放缓期间刺激活动。
*   **充分就业**: 通过创造就业机会减少失业。
*   **价格稳定**: 防止高通胀或通缩。
*   **收入再分配**: 利用税收和支出来减少不平等。

这些目标通过**两个主要工具**来实现：
*   **税收**: 调整税率（例如所得税、公司税或销售税）以影响个人和企业的支出能力。
*   **政府支出**: 为基础设施、教育、国防或社会福利等项目分配资金。

美国财政部执行这些政策，通常通过总统提出并经国会批准的年度预算来实施。

### 1.3.3 财政政策类型（Types of Fiscal Policy）: Expansionary vs. Contractionary

**English:**
Fiscal policy can be **expansionary** (to boost the economy) or **contractionary** (to cool it down). The choice depends on the economic cycle—boom times vs. recessions.
*   **Expansionary Fiscal Policy**: This involves increasing government spending or cutting taxes to inject more money into the economy, raising aggregate demand (total spending by consumers, businesses, and government). It's used when the economy is weak, such as during high unemployment or low growth.
*   **Contractionary Fiscal Policy**: This means decreasing spending or raising taxes to reduce aggregate demand, which helps control inflation during economic booms.

**Example**: Imagine the economy as a car engine. Expansionary policy is like pressing the gas pedal to speed up (e.g., more spending on roads creates jobs). Contractionary policy is like easing off the gas to avoid overheating (e.g., higher taxes reduce excessive consumer spending).

**中文:**
财政政策可以是**扩张性的**（以提振经济）或**紧缩性的**（以冷却经济）。选择取决于经济周期——繁荣时期还是衰退时期。
*   **扩张性财政政策**: 涉及增加政府支出或减税，向经济注入更多资金，提高总需求（消费者、企业和政府的总支出）。当经济疲软时（例如高失业率或低增长期间）使用。
*   **紧缩性财政政策**: 意味着减少支出或增税以降低总需求，这有助于在经济繁荣期间控制通胀。

**示例**: 将经济想象成汽车引擎。扩张性政策就像踩油门加速（例如，增加道路支出创造就业机会）。紧缩性政策就像松开油门以避免过热（例如，提高税收减少过度的消费支出）。

### 1.3.4 逆周期调节（Countercyclical Regulation）: The Key Stabilizing Logic

**English:**
*   A central principle in the U.S. Fiscal policy is **countercyclical regulation** (also called countercyclical fiscal policy). This means the government acts in the opposite direction of the economic cycle to stabilize it:
*   During a **recession** (economic downturn), the government adopts expansionary measures to counteract falling demand.
*   During an **expansion** (economic boom), it shifts to contractionary measures to prevent overheating, like bubbles in asset prices or runaway inflation.

This logic helps avoid extreme swings, promoting steady growth. Automatic stabilizers (built-in features like unemployment benefits that rise during recessions) enhance this, but discretionary actions (deliberate policy changes) are often needed.

> **Specific Case Study: The 2008-2009 Global Financial Crisis**
>
> **Context**: The U.S. The economy entered a severe recession due to the housing market collapse and banking failures. Unemployment soared to 10%, and GDP (gross domestic product) shrank by about 4%.
>
> **Countercyclical Response**: Under President Obama, Congress passed the American Recovery and Reinvestment Act (ARRA) in 2009, an expansionary package worth $831 billion. This included:
> Tax cuts for individuals and businesses to increase disposable income (e.g., payroll tax reductions, putting more money in workers' pockets).
> Increased spending on infrastructure (e.g., roads, bridges, and schools), creating jobs in construction.
> Extended unemployment benefits, acting as an automatic stabilizer to support consumer spending.
>
> **Outcome**: This helped shorten the recession, adding millions of jobs and boosting GDP growth. Without it, economists estimate unemployment could have hit 12-15%. It exemplified countercyclical logic: When private sector demand collapsed, government stepped in to fill the gap.

> **Another Example: The COVID-19 Pandemic Response (2020-2021)**
>
> **Context**: The pandemic caused a sharp economic shutdown, with unemployment jumping to 14.8% in April 2020 and businesses closing en masse.
>
> **Countercyclical Measures**: Multiple stimulus bills were enacted, totaling over $5 trillion:
> The CARES Act (2020) under President Trump provided direct payments ($1,200 per adult) to boost consumer spending, expanded unemployment insurance (adding $600 weekly), and loans to small businesses via the Paycheck Protection Program (PPP).
> The American Rescue Plan (2021) under President Biden added more direct payments ($1,400), child tax credits, and funding for vaccines and schools.
>
> **Logic in Action**: These expansionary steps countered the cycle by maintaining demand when private activity halted. For instance, direct payments helped families pay bills, preventing deeper poverty and supporting retail sales. Result: The economy recovered faster than expected, with unemployment dropping to 3.9% by late 2021.

**中文:**
*   美国财政政策的一个核心原则是**逆周期调节**（也称为逆周期财政政策）。这意味着政府采取与经济周期相反的行动来稳定经济：
*   在**衰退**（经济低迷）期间，政府采取扩张性措施以抵消需求下降。
*   在**扩张**（经济繁荣）期间，政府转向紧缩性措施以防止过热，如资产价格泡沫或失控的通胀。

这种逻辑有助于避免极端波动，促进稳定增长。自动稳定器（如在衰退期间增加的失业救济金等内置功能）增强了这一点，但通常也需要相机抉择的行动（深思熟虑的政策变更）。

> **具体案例研究：2008-2009 年全球金融危机**
>
> **背景**: 由于房地产市场崩溃和银行倒闭，美国经济陷入严重衰退。失业率飙升至 10%，GDP 萎缩约 4%。
>
> **逆周期响应**: 在奥巴马总统领导下，国会于 2009 年通过了《美国复苏与再投资法案》(ARRA)，这是一个价值 8310 亿美元的扩张性计划。其中包括：
> 对个人和企业的减税，以增加可支配收入（例如，减少工资税，让工人口袋里有更多钱）。
> 增加基础设施支出（例如道路、桥梁和学校），在建筑业创造就业机会。
> 延长失业救济金，充当支持消费支出的自动稳定器。
>
> **结果**: 这有助于缩短衰退期，增加了数百万个就业机会并提振了 GDP 增长。如果没有它，经济学家估计失业率可能达到 12-15%。这体现了逆周期逻辑：当私营部门需求崩溃时，政府介入填补缺口。

> **另一个例子：COVID-19 大流行应对 (2020-2021)**
>
> **背景**: 大流行导致经济急剧停摆，2020 年 4 月失业率跃升至 14.8%，企业大量倒闭。
>
> **逆周期措施**: 制定了多项刺激法案，总额超过 5 万亿美元：
> 特朗普总统领导下的《CARES 法案》(2020) 提供了直接付款（每位成人 1,200 美元）以提振消费支出，扩大了失业保险（每周增加 600 美元），并通过薪资保护计划 (PPP) 向小企业提供贷款。
> 拜登总统领导下的《美国救援计划》(2021) 增加了更多直接付款（1,400 美元）、儿童税收抵免以及疫苗和学校资金。
>
> **逻辑运作**: 这些扩张性步骤通过在私人活动停止时维持需求来对抗周期。例如，直接付款帮助家庭支付账单，防止更严重的贫困并支持零售销售。结果：经济复苏快于预期，失业率在 2021 年底降至 3.9%。

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image14.png>)

*Graph: Fiscal deficit and unemployment rate / 图表：财政赤字与失业率*

### 1.3.5 财政政策逻辑中的挑战与考量 (Challenges and Considerations in Fiscal Policy Logic)

**English:**
While effective, fiscal policy isn't perfect:
*   **Timing and Political Delays**: Policies take time to implement; Congress debates can slow responses.
*   **Debt Implications**: Expansionary policies often increase the national debt. The logic is that short-term borrowing is justified if it prevents long-term damage, but sustained deficits can lead to higher interest rates.
*   **Coordination with Monetary Policy**: Fiscal actions work best alongside Fed policies, like low interest rates during recessions.

**中文:**
虽然有效，但财政政策并不完美：
*   **时机和政治延误**: 政策实施需要时间；国会辩论可能会减慢反应速度。
*   **债务影响**: 扩张性政策通常会增加国债。其逻辑是，如果短期借款能防止长期损害，那就是合理的，但持续赤字可能导致利率上升。
*   **与货币政策的协调**: 财政行动与美联储政策（如衰退期间的低利率）配合使用效果最好。

## 1.4 货币、财政政策背后的逻辑，以及不同经济环境/政策机制下的资产绩效 (The Logic Behind Monetary and Fiscal Policy, and Asset performance Under Different Economic Environment/Policy Regime)

**English:**
The focus is on the underlying logic of monetary/fiscal policy and their implications for key assets: U.S. equities (represented by the S\&P 500) and U.S. Treasuries (focusing on the 10-Year Treasury yield and bond prices). Rate changes do not have uniform effects; outcomes depend on economic contexts, such as soft landings versus recessions.

**中文:**
重点在于货币/财政政策的底层逻辑及其对关键资产的影响：美国股票（以标准普尔 500 指数为代表）和美国国债（侧重于 10 年期国债收益率和债券价格）。利率变化的影响并不统一；结果取决于经济背景，例如软着陆还是衰退。

### 1.4.1 美国应对经济周期波动的政策（U.S. Policy Responses to Business Cycle Fluctuations）

#### **Monetary Policy (Federal Reserve) / 货币政策（美联储）**
*   **Expansion/Inflationary Pressures (扩张/通胀压力)**: Fed hikes the federal funds rate to increase borrowing costs, cool economic growth, and curb inflation (e.g., 2022–2023 rate hikes: 0.25% to 5.25% to combat post-COVID inflation). QT (reducing Fed’s bond holdings) is used to tighten liquidity, pressuring long-term bond yields and equity valuations.
    *   美联储提高联邦基金利率以增加借贷成本，冷却经济增长并抑制通胀（例如，2022-2023 年加息：0.25% 至 5.25% 以对抗后 COVID 通胀）。QT（减少美联储债券持有量）用于紧缩流动性，对长期债券收益率和股票估值施压。
*   **Contraction/Recession (收缩/衰退)**: Fed cuts the federal funds rate to lower borrowing costs, stimulate spending and investment (e.g., 2020 rate cut to near 0% during COVID recession). QE (purchasing Treasury and mortgage-backed securities) injects liquidity, lowers long-term yields, and supports asset prices.
    *   美联储降低联邦基金利率以降低借贷成本，刺激支出和投资（例如，2020 年 COVID 衰退期间降息至接近 0%）。QE（购买国债和抵押贷款支持证券）注入流动性，降低长期收益率并支持资产价格。

#### **Fiscal Policy (U.S. Government) / 财政政策（美国政府）**
*   **Contraction/Recession (收缩/衰退)**: Expansionary fiscal policy—tax cuts (e.g., 2017 Tax Cuts and Jobs Act) and increased government spending (e.g., 2020 CARES Act, $2.2 trillion in stimulus) to boost aggregate demand. This increases U.S. Treasury issuance, potentially pressuring bond yields if supply outpaces demand.
    *   扩张性财政政策——减税（例如 2017 年《减税和就业法案》）和增加政府支出（例如 2020 年 CARES 法案，2.2 万亿美元刺激计划）以提振总需求。这增加了美国国债的发行，如果供应超过需求，可能会对债券收益率造成压力。
*   **Expansion/High Debt (扩张/高债务)**: Restrictive fiscal policy (rarely used in practice) – tax hikes or spending cuts to reduce budget deficits. The U.S. often runs deficits even during expansions, impacting long-term Treasury supply.
    *   限制性财政政策（实践中很少使用）——增税或削减支出以减少预算赤字。美国即使在扩张期间也经常出现赤字，影响长期国债供应。

### 1.4.2 美国金融市场在整个经济周期中的表现（U.S. Financial Market Performance Across the Business Cycle）

**English:**
Critical for investment decisions—asset class returns vary systematically across cycle stages.

**中文:**
对投资决策至关重要——资产类别的回报在不同周期阶段存在系统性差异。

#### **膨胀阶段 Expansion Stage**
*   **Stocks**: Outperform other assets. CFA Curriculum notes growth stocks (e.g., technology, consumer discretionary) tend to lead as corporate profits rise—S\&P 500 average annual return: 14%–16% during expansions.
    *   **股票**: 表现优于其他资产。CFA 课程指出，随着企业利润上升，成长型股票（如科技、非必需消费品）往往领涨——扩张期间标准普尔 500 指数的平均年回报率：14%–16%。
*   **Bonds**: Moderate returns. Short-term bonds outperform long-term bonds as the Fed may hike rates to curb inflation.
    *   **债券**: 回报温和。由于美联储可能加息以抑制通胀，短期债券表现优于长期债券。
*   **Commodities**: Strong returns. Industrial metals (e.g., copper) benefit from increased business investment; energy prices rise with consumer and industrial demand.
    *   **大宗商品**: 回报强劲。工业金属（如铜）受益于商业投资增加；能源价格随消费和工业需求上涨。

#### **高峰至收缩（衰退）阶段 Peak to Contraction (Recession) Stage**
*   **Stocks**: Decline sharply. Defensive stocks (consumer staples, healthcare, utilities) outperform as investors seek stability. S\&P 500 average annual return: -18% to -22% during recessions.
    *   **股票**: 大幅下跌。随着投资者寻求稳定，防御性股票（必需消费品、医疗保健、公用事业）表现优异。衰退期间标准普尔 500 指数的平均年回报率：-18% 至 -22%。
*   **Bonds**: Safe-haven assets. Long-term U.S. Treasuries outperform as the Fed cuts rates—10-year Treasury average yield falls by 1.4%–1.6 percentage points during recessions; prices rise accordingly.
    *   **债券**: 避险资产。随着美联储降息，长期美国国债表现优异——衰退期间 10 年期国债平均收益率下降 1.4%–1.6 个百分点；价格相应上涨。
*   **Commodities**: Underperform. Demand for industrial commodities collapses; energy prices drop amid reduced consumption.
    *   **大宗商品**: 表现不佳。工业大宗商品需求崩溃；能源价格因消费减少而下跌。

#### **从低谷期到早期扩张阶段 Trough to Early Expansion Stage**
*   **Stocks**: Rebound strongly. Cyclical stocks (e.g., industrials, financials) lead as economic optimism returns—S\&P 500 average return in the first 12 months post-trough: 23%–27% (CFA textbook data).
    *   **股票**: 强劲反弹。随着经济乐观情绪回归，周期性股票（如工业、金融）领涨——谷底后前 12 个月标准普尔 500 指数的平均回报率：23%–27%（CFA 教材数据）。
*   **Bonds**: Still positive but underperform stocks. Short-term bonds gain as rates stabilize; long-term bonds return moderate.
    *   **债券**: 仍为正值但表现逊于股票。随着利率企稳，短期债券上涨；长期债券回报温和。
*   **Commodities**: Rebound. Industrial metals lead as business investment resumes; energy prices rise with economic activity.
    *   **大宗商品**: 反弹。随着商业投资恢复，工业金属领涨；能源价格随经济活动上涨。

### 1.4.3 货币政策对美国股市的影响 Impacts of monetary policy on U.S. Equities (S\&P 500)

**English:**
Equities are sensitive to rate changes due to effects on corporate earnings, discount rates, and investor sentiment.
*   **During Rate Hikes:** Stocks often face headwinds as higher borrowing costs compress profit margins and slow growth. The S\&P 500 averaged monthly returns of 0.88% from 1991-2015, but performance varied; in rising rate periods, returns were higher (1.26%) than in declining periods (0.73%), though volatility increased. In 2022, amid hikes, the S\&P 500 fell nearly 20%. However, early-stage hikes can see continued rallies if fundamentals remain strong. (Source: Mackenzie Investments)
*   **During Rate Cuts**: Equities generally perform well in non-recessionary environments, with average 1-year returns of almost 20% post-first cut in soft landings. In recessionary cuts, returns can turn negative. Since 1974, the S\&P 500 rallied in most 1-year periods post-first cut, but fell during recessions (e.g., 2001, 2008). In 2024-2025 cuts, the S\&P 500 gained amid constructive growth. (Source: Mackenzie Investments)

**中文:**
由于对企业盈利、贴现率和投资者情绪的影响，股票对利率变化敏感。
*   **加息期间**: 股票通常面临阻力，因为较高的借贷成本压缩了利润率并减缓了增长。1991-2015 年，标准普尔 500 指数的平均月回报率为 0.88%，但表现各异；在利率上升期间，回报率 (1.26%) 高于下降期间 (0.73%)，尽管波动性增加。2022 年，在加息背景下，标准普尔 500 指数下跌近 20%。然而，如果基本面保持强劲，早期加息阶段可能会出现持续反弹。
*   **降息期间**: 股票在非衰退环境中通常表现良好，在软着陆的首次降息后，平均 1 年回报率接近 20%。在衰退性降息中，回报可能会变为负值。自 1974 年以来，标准普尔 500 指数在大多数首次降息后的 1 年期间反弹，但在衰退期间下跌（例如 2001 年、2008 年）。在 2024-2025 年的降息中，标准普尔 500 指数在建设性增长中上涨。

### 1.4.4 货币政策对美国国债的影响 Impacts of monetary on U.S. Treasuries (10-Year Yield and Bonds)

**English:**
Bonds move inversely to yields; rate changes affect prices and the yield curve.
*   **During Rate Hikes:** Bond prices fall as yields rise to compete with new issues. The 10-Year Treasury yield increased by an average of 53 basis points during the last four hike cycles. In 2022, yields surged from around 1.5% to over 4%, causing bond losses.
*   **During Rate Cuts**: Bonds rally as yields fall, especially longer-duration ones. The yield curve often steepens, with short-end yields dropping more.

**中文:**
债券与收益率反向变动；利率变化影响价格和收益率曲线。
*   **加息期间**: 债券价格下跌，因为收益率上升以与新发行债券竞争。在过去四个加息周期中，10 年期国债收益率平均上升了 53 个基点。2022 年，收益率从 1.5% 左右飙升至 4% 以上，导致债券损失。
*   **降息期间**: 债券随着收益率下降而反弹，尤其是久期较长的债券。收益率曲线通常变陡，短端收益率下降更多。

### 1.4.5 情景分析：软着陆vs.经济衰退 (Scenario Analysis: Soft Landing vs. Recession)

**English:**
Rate effects vary by context—cuts in a soft landing (gradual slowdown without recession) differ from those in a recession.
In soft landings, both assets benefit; in recessions, bonds provide diversification as equities weaken.

**中文:**
利率效应因背景而异——软着陆（没有衰退的逐渐放缓）中的降息与衰退中的降息不同。
在软着陆中，两种资产都受益；在衰退中，随着股票走弱，债券提供多样化。

| Scenario (情景) | Description (描述) | Fed Action (美联储行动) | Impact on U.S. Equities (对美股影响) | Impact on U.S. Treasuries (对美债影响) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Soft Landing (软着陆)** | The economy slows down but avoids contraction; inflation cools Examples: 1995, 2019 cycles<br>经济放缓但避免收缩；通胀降温。例如：1995、2019 周期 | Gradual rate cuts (e.g., 75-200 bps over a period of time)<br>逐步降息（例如一段时间内降 75-200 个基点） | **Positive**: Lower interest rates boost corporate earnings; S\&P 500 posts an average 20% gain in the first year after rate cuts. Small-cap stocks and cyclical stocks outperform.<br>**正面**: 较低的利率提振企业盈利；标准普尔 500 指数在降息后第一年平均上涨 20%。小盘股和周期股表现优异。 | Rally in intermediate/long-term bonds; yields fall modestly (e.g., 10-Year Treasury yield drops by 0.5-1%); yield curve steepens.<br>中长期债券反弹；收益率适度下降（例如 10 年期国债收益率下降 0.5-1%）；收益率曲线变陡。 |
| **Recession (衰退)** | Sharp economic downturn; rising unemployment rate and falling GDP. Examples: 2001, 2008 cycles<br>经济急剧下滑；失业率上升，GDP 下降。例如：2001、2008 周期 | Aggressive rate cuts (e.g., more than 200 bps in a rapid manner)<br>激进降息（例如快速降超过 200 个基点） | **Negative or mixed**: Corporate earnings decline; S\&P 500 often records a drop<br>**负面或混合**: 企业盈利下降；标准普尔 500 指数通常录得下跌 | **Strong rally**: yields plummet sharply (e.g., 10-Year Treasury yield drops by over 1%); bonds act as a risk hedge; yield curve steepens sharply.<br>**强劲反弹**: 收益率急剧暴跌（例如 10 年期国债收益率下降超过 1%）；债券充当风险对冲；收益率曲线急剧变陡。 |

## 1.5 历史案例研究（Historical Case Studies）

> **Specific Case Study: 1994-1995 Hike Cycle: Fed raised rates from 3% to 6%; S\&P 500 gained 37.4% during the cycle, but 10-Year yield rose 63 bps. Soft landing achieved.**
> **具体案例研究：1994-1995 加息周期：美联储将利率从 3% 提高到 6%；标准普尔 500 指数在周期内上涨 37.4%，但 10 年期收益率上升 63 个基点。实现软着陆。**

### 1.5.1 结论 (Conclusion)

**English:**
Understanding Fed rate logic requires contextual analysis: hikes curb excesses, cuts support recovery, but implications for U.S. Stocks and bonds hinge on whether the economy achieves a soft landing or enters recession. Employees should advise clients based on these scenarios, emphasizing diversification (e.g., bonds as a hedge in downturns). Monitor economic indicators like unemployment (below 4.6% signals potential soft landing) and yield curve inversions for signals.

**中文:**
理解美联储利率逻辑需要背景分析：加息抑制过度，降息支持复苏，但对美股和债券的影响取决于经济是实现软着陆还是进入衰退。员工应根据这些情景为客户提供建议，强调多样化（例如，债券作为低迷时期的对冲）。监控失业率（低于 4.6% 预示潜在软着陆）和收益率曲线倒挂等经济指标以获取信号。

## 1.6 以下例题（Practice Questions）

**Q1. Which indicator is classified as a coincident indicator?**
*   A. ISM Manufacturing PMI
*   B. Nonfarm Payrolls (NFP)
*   C. Unemployment Rate
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: Coincident indicators change with the economic cycle and directly reflect current activity. Nonfarm Payrolls (NFP) is a key coincident indicator, while ISM Manufacturing PMI is leading and Unemployment Rate is lagging.

**Q2. Which market participant acts as a primary market maker in the USD-related FX spot trading?**
*   A. Institutional Investors
*   B. Commercial Banks
*   C. Multinational Corporations (MNCs)
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: Major U.S. commercial banks (e.g., JPMorgan Chase) dominate USD-related spot trading as primary market makers, accounting for ~40% of global USD FX volume.

**Q3. What is the core objective of expansionary fiscal policy during a recession?**
*   A. Reduce government spending
*   B. Boost aggregate demand
*   C. Raise tax rates
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: Expansionary fiscal policy uses tax cuts or increased government spending to inject money into the economy, raising aggregate demand to counteract recessionary pressures.

**Q4. If the Fed implements a contractionary monetary policy (rate hikes), how is the 10-Year Treasury yield likely to respond?**
*   A. Rise
*   B. Fall
*   C. Remain unchanged
> **Answer: A**
> **Explanation**: When the Fed raises interest rates, short‑term rates increase. This action often transmits to longer‑term rates through shifts in market expectations and inflation outlooks, pushing the 10‑Year Treasury yield higher. An increase in the policy rate tends to lift the entire yield curve.

**Q5. Which asset class typically outperforms during the peak-to-contraction (recession) stage of the business cycle?**
*   A. Growth stocks
*   B. Long-term U.S. Treasuries
*   C. Industrial commodities
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: During recessions, long-term Treasuries act as safe-havens—yields fall sharply (e.g., 10-Year yield dropped over 300 bps in 2007-2008), boosting bond prices.

**Q6. How does a stronger U.S. dollar (USD) impact U.S. net exports?**
*   A. Increases export competitiveness
*   B. Reduces export competitiveness
*   C. Has no impact
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: A stronger USD makes U.S. goods more expensive for foreign buyers, reducing export competitiveness. For example, the 2022 USD strength weighed on U.S. export growth.

**Q7. If the Consumer Confidence Index (CCI) drops to 75, what does this signal for consumer spending?**
*   A. Strong spending intent
*   B. Caution and weakened spending
*   C. No change in spending patterns
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: A CCI below 80 indicates consumer caution, which weakens discretionary spending (e.g., autos, electronics), a key driver of U.S. GDP (68-70%)

**Q8. on 2026 Jan 13, U.S. 2025 Dec's inflation data was released, and the core inflation rate was slightly lower than the market expectation, which asset has the highest probability of going up?**
*   A. Gold
*   B. 10-year U.S. government bond ETF
*   C. Hang seng Index
> **Answer: B**
> **Explanation**: Bonds benefits most when inflation is lower than expected.

# 2. 股票（Stock）

> **考试小贴士 (Exam Tips)**：股票章节题目占比约为15%左右。
> The Stock chapter accounts for about 15% of the questions.
>
> *   2.1、2.2、2.3考察概念的掌握情况，考试主要覆盖概念题。
>     2.1, 2.2, and 2.3 test the mastery of concepts; the exam mainly covers conceptual questions.
> *   2.4公司行为部分为重点考察内容，会考察概念及灵活运用，例如你需要知道公司为什么要进行股票分割以及这样做会带来什么影响，如何在MooMoo App中找到股息、股票分割及股票回购的相关信息也会进行操作层面考察。
>     2.4 Corporate Actions is a key examination area, covering concepts and flexible application. For example, you need to know why a company splits its stock and the impact. Finding information on dividends, stock splits, and buybacks in the MooMoo App will also be tested on an operational level.
> *   2.5股票资产的常见指标全部为重点考察内容，员工需要掌握概念、如何运用以及MooMoo App中的相关操作，例如ROE是什么、如何计算以及在MooMoo App中如何去找。
>     2.5 Common Stock Metrics are all key examination content. Employees need to master concepts, application, and relevant operations in the MooMoo App, such as what ROE is, how to calculate it, and how to find it in the App.
> *   **产品层面**：如何在MooMoo App中找到股息、股票分割及股票回购的相关信息/常见股票指标在MooMoo App中的查找路径。
>     **Product Level**: How to find information on dividends, stock splits, and buybacks in the MooMoo App / search paths for common stock metrics in the App.

## 2.1 主要市场差异 (Key Market Distinctions)

### 一级市场与二级市场 (Primary vs. Secondary Markets)

*   **Primary Market (一级市场)**:
    *   **English**: Where companies "sell new stocks/bonds for the first time" (like a brand-new car sale). E.g., Airbnb’s 2020 IPO (Initial Public Offering) on NASDAQ—they sold new shares to investors and raised $3.5B.
    *   **中文**: 公司“首次出售新股票/债券”的地方（就像新车销售）。例如，Airbnb 2020 年在纳斯达克的 IPO（首次公开募股）——他们向投资者出售新股并筹集了 35 亿美元。

*   **Secondary Market (二级市场)**:
    *   **English**: Where investors trade "used" stocks/bonds (like a used car sale). E.g., when you buy Apple stock on Nasdaq, you’re buying it from another investor, not Apple itself.
    *   **中文**: 投资者交易“二手”股票/债券的地方（就像二手车销售）。例如，当你在纳斯达克购买苹果股票时，你是从另一个投资者那里购买，而不是从苹果公司本身购买。

## 2.2 普通股与优先股（股票的两种主要类型）Common vs. Preferred Stocks (The Two Main Types of Stocks)

**English:**
When you buy a stock, you’re buying a "piece of the company." Common and preferred stocks are like two different "memberships" in the company—they have different rights.

**中文:**
当你购买股票时，你购买的是“公司的一部分”。普通股和优先股就像公司里的两种不同“会员资格”——它们拥有不同的权利。

| Feature (What Matters to You) / 特征（你在乎什么） | Common Shares (Most Popular) / 普通股（最流行） | Preferred Shares (Less Common) / 优先股（较少见） |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Voting Rights (Can You Vote on Company Decisions?)**<br>**投票权（你能对公司决策投票吗？）** | **Yes**. E.g., Apple common shareholders vote on who sits on Apple’s board of directors (the people who run the company).<br>**有**。例如，苹果普通股股东投票选举谁进入苹果董事会（管理公司的人）。 | **No (or very limited)**. You can’t vote on company decisions—you get other benefits instead.<br>**无（或非常有限）**。你不能对公司决策投票——取而代之的是你获得其他利益。 |
| **Dividends (Do You Get Regular Payments?)**<br>**股息（你能获得定期付款吗？）** | **Variable (not guaranteed)**. The company decides how much to pay (if any).<br>**可变（不保证）**。公司决定支付多少（如果有的话）。 | **Fixed (guaranteed, if the company pays dividends)**.<br>**固定（如果公司支付股息，则是保证的）**。 |
| **Liquidation Priority (What If the Company Fails?)**<br>**清算优先级（如果公司倒闭怎么办？）** | **Lower**. If the company goes bankrupt, debt holders (people who lent the company money) and preferred shareholders get paid first—common shareholders get whatever is left (if anything).<br>**较低**。如果公司破产，债权人（借钱给公司的人）和优先股股东先获得偿付——普通股股东获得剩余的任何东西（如果有的话）。 | **Higher**. You get paid before common shareholders if the company fails (but after debt holders).<br>**较高**。如果公司倒闭，你在普通股股东之前获得偿付（但在债权人之后）。 |

## 2.3 美国存托凭证（ADRs）：无需离开美国就能购买外国股票 (ADRs: Buy Foreign Stocks Without Leaving the U.S.)

**English:**
**ADR** = American Depositary Receipt. It’s a "proxy" for foreign stocks—traded on U.S. Exchanges (NYSE/NASDAQ) in U.S. dollars, so you don’t need to buy foreign currency or use foreign brokers.
*   **Simple Explanation**: A U.S. bank buys shares of a foreign company (e.g., Alibaba in China), then issues ADRs (1 ADR = a certain number of foreign shares) to U.S. investors.
*   **Key Benefit**: Easy to invest in foreign companies (like Alibaba) using the same U.S. brokerage account you use for Apple or Amazon.

**中文:**
**ADR** = 美国存托凭证。它是外国股票的“代理”——在美国交易所 (NYSE/NASDAQ) 以美元交易，因此你无需购买外币或使用外国经纪商。
*   **简单解释**: 一家美国银行购买外国公司（例如中国的阿里巴巴）的股票，然后向美国投资者发行 ADR（1 份 ADR = 一定数量的外国股票）。
*   **主要好处**: 使用你用于购买 Apple 或 Amazon 的同一个美国经纪账户，即可轻松投资外国公司（如阿里巴巴）。

**Example:** BABA

## 2.4 公司行为 (Corporate Action)

### 2.4.1 股息 (Dividends)

**English:**
*   **What Are Dividends?**
    Dividends are payments that a company makes to its shareholders from its profits. They are like a share of the company's earnings distributed to owners of the stock. Not all companies pay dividends—many growth-focused companies reinvest profits instead—but mature, profitable companies often do. Dividends are usually paid quarterly (every three months), but can also be semi-annual or annual.

*   **Key Things to Know:**
    *   **Types of Dividends**: The most common is cash dividends (money paid directly to shareholders). There are also stock dividends (additional shares instead of cash).
    *   **Ex-Dividend Date**: This is the cutoff date to be eligible for the next dividend. If you buy the stock on or after this date, you won't get the dividend—you must own it before.
    *   **Dividend Yield**: This is the annual dividend amount divided by the stock price, shown as a percentage. It tells investors how much return they're getting from dividends alone.
    *   **Why Companies Pay Dividends**: To reward shareholders and signal financial health. It can attract income-focused investors, like retirees.
    *   **Impact on Investors**: Dividends provide regular income. They're taxed as ordinary income or at a lower rate if qualified (held for over 60 days). Reinvesting dividends can compound growth over time.

**中文:**
*   **什么是股息？**
    股息是公司从利润中向股东支付的款项。它们就像是分配给股票所有者的公司收益份额。并非所有公司都支付股息——许多注重增长的公司会进行利润再投资——但成熟、盈利的公司通常会支付。股息通常按季度（每三个月）支付，但也可能是半年度或年度支付。

*   **你需要知道的关键事项：**
    *   **股息类型**: 最常见的是现金股息（直接支付给股东的钱）。也有股票股息（额外的股票而不是现金）。
    *   **除息日**: 这是获得下一次股息资格的截止日期。如果你在此日期或之后购买股票，你将不会获得股息——你必须在此之前持有它。
    *   **股息收益率**: 这是年度股息金额除以股票价格，以百分比显示。它告诉投资者他们仅从股息中获得了多少回报。
    *   **公司为什么支付股息**: 奖励股东并表明财务健康。它可以吸引注重收入的投资者，如退休人员。
    *   **对投资者的影响**: 股息提供定期收入。它们按普通收入征税，或者如果是合格股息（持有超过 60 天），则按较低税率征税。再投资股息可以随时间推移实现复利增长。

**How to find dividend record, dividend amount, dividend yield and important dividend data in MooMoo App:**
Select a company——公司——分红派息——派息统计、派息记录

| Image 1 | Image 2 |
| :--- | :--- |
| ![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-1.png>) | ![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-3.png>) |

*   **Example: Apple Inc. (AAPL)**
    *   **English**: Apple, a major U.S. tech company, resumed paying dividends in 2012 after a long pause. As of January 2026, Apple's annual dividend is $1.04 per share, paid quarterly (e.g., $0.26 per share in November 2025). This gives a dividend yield of about 0.40% based on recent stock prices around $260. For instance, if you own 100 shares, you'd receive $104 annually (before taxes).
    *   **中文**: Apple 是一家主要的美国科技公司，在长期暂停后于 2012 年恢复支付股息。截至 2026 年 1 月，Apple 的年度股息为每股 1.04 美元，按季度支付（例如，2025 年 11 月每股 0.26 美元）。基于近期约 260 美元的股价，股息收益率约为 0.40%。例如，如果你拥有 100 股，你每年将获得 104 美元（税前）。

### 2.4.2 股票分割 (Stock Splits)

**English:**
*   **What Are Stock Splits?**
    A stock split is when a company increases the number of its shares by dividing existing ones. For example, in a 2-for-1 split, each share becomes two, and the price halves. The total value of your investment stays the same right after the split—it's like cutting a pizza into more slices without adding more pizza.

*   **Key Things to Know:**
    *   **Common Ratios**: 2-for-1, 3-for-1, or 4-for-1 are typical. Reverse splits (e.g., 1-for-10) combine shares to raise the price, often for low-priced stocks.
    *   **Why Companies Do Splits**: To make shares more affordable and attract more investors. High stock prices (e.g., over $500) can seem expensive, so splitting lowers the per-share price without changing the company's value.
    *   **Impact on Investors**: No immediate gain or loss in value, but it can increase liquidity (easier trading) and sometimes boost stock price due to positive perception. Dividends and earnings per share adjust accordingly.
    *   **Announcement and Effective Date**: Companies announce splits in advance; the split happens on the effective date, and prices adjust automatically.

**中文:**
*   **什么是股票分割？**
    股票分割是指公司通过拆分现有股份来增加其股份数量。例如，在 2 拆 1 的分割中，每股变成两股，价格减半。分割后你的投资总价值保持不变——这就像把披萨切成更多片，但没有增加更多披萨。

*   **你需要知道的关键事项：**
    *   **常见比例**: 2 拆 1、3 拆 1 或 4 拆 1 是典型的。反向分割（例如 1 并 10）合并股份以提高价格，通常用于低价股。
    *   **公司为什么进行分割**: 为了使股票更实惠并吸引更多投资者。高股价（例如超过 500 美元）看起来可能很昂贵，因此分割降低了每股价格而不改变公司的价值。
    *   **对投资者的影响**: 价值没有立即收益或损失，但它可以增加流动性（更容易交易），有时由于积极的看法而推高股价。股息和每股收益相应调整。
    *   **公告日和生效日**: 公司提前宣布分割；分割在生效日发生，价格自动调整。

**How to find stock split/reverse split information in MooMoo App:**
Select a company——公司——拆股并股

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-img_v3_02ua_7629f028-6d82-4976-af2b-f82de23b128g.jpg>)

**Example: Apple Inc. (AAPL) 2020 Stock Split**
*   **English**: The most recent was a 4-for-1 split on August 31, 2020. Before the split, Apple's stock was trading around $500 per share. After, it became about $125 per share, and shareholders got three additional shares for each one held. For example, if you owned 100 shares at $500 ($50,000 total), post-split you'd have 400 shares at $125 ($50,000 total). This made Apple more accessible to retail investors and contributed to increased trading volume afterward.
*   **中文**: 最近一次是在 2020 年 8 月 31 日进行的 4 拆 1 分割。分割前，Apple 的股票交易价格约为每股 500 美元。之后，它变成了约每股 125 美元，股东每持有一股就获得三股额外股份。例如，如果你拥有 100 股，每股 500 美元（总计 50,000 美元），分割后你将拥有 400 股，每股 125 美元（总计 50,000 美元）。这使得散户投资者更容易接触到 Apple，并促进了随后的交易量增加。

### 2.4.3 股票回购 (Stock Buybacks)

**English:**
*   **What Are Stock Buybacks?**
    Stock buybacks (or share repurchases) occur when a company uses its cash to buy back its own shares from the market. This reduces the number of outstanding shares, effectively giving remaining shareholders a larger ownership stake.

*   **Key Things to Know:**
    *   **How It Works**: Companies announce a buyback program with a set amount (e.g., $10 billion) and timeframe. They buy shares on the open market or through private deals.
    *   **Why Companies Do Buybacks**: To return cash to shareholders (like dividends but more flexible), boost earnings per share (EPS) by spreading profits over fewer shares, and signal that management thinks the stock is undervalued.
    *   **Impact on Investors**: Can increase stock price over time due to higher EPS and perceived confidence. It's tax-efficient (no immediate tax like dividends) but doesn't provide cash unless you sell shares. However, it can be controversial if done instead of investing in growth.
    *   **Authorization**: Boards approve programs, but companies aren't required to complete them fully.

**中文:**
*   **什么是股票回购？**
    股票回购是指公司使用现金从市场上回购自己的股票。这减少了流通股的数量，实际上给了剩余股东更大的所有权比例。

*   **你需要知道的关键事项：**
    *   **运作方式**: 公司宣布一项具有设定金额（例如 100 亿美元）和时间框架的回购计划。他们在公开市场上或通过私人交易购买股票。
    *   **公司为什么进行回购**: 向股东返还现金（像股息一样但更灵活），通过将利润分摊到更少的股份上来提高每股收益 (EPS)，并表明管理层认为股票被低估了。
    *   **对投资者的影响**: 由于更高的 EPS 和感知到的信心，随着时间的推移可能会推高股价。它具有税收效率（不像股息那样立即征税），但除非你出售股票，否则不会提供现金。然而，如果这样做是为了代替投资增长，可能会引起争议。
    *   **授权**: 董事会批准计划，但公司不被要求完全完成它们。

**Example: Apple Inc. (AAPL) Buyback Program**
*   **English**: Apple has one of the largest buyback programs in history. Over the past decade (ending around 2025), Apple repurchased about $704 billion worth of its shares. In May 2024, it announced a record $110 billion buyback, followed by another $100 billion in May 2025. This reduced outstanding shares from about 25 billion in 2015 to around 14.84 billion by 2025, boosting EPS by about 2.62% annually from buybacks alone. For investors, this meant each remaining share represented a bigger slice of Apple's profits, helping drive long-term stock appreciation.
*   **中文**: Apple 拥有历史上最大的回购计划之一。在过去十年（截至 2025 年左右），Apple 回购了价值约 7040 亿美元的股票。2024 年 5 月，它宣布了创纪录的 1100 亿美元回购，随后在 2025 年 5 月又宣布了 1000 亿美元。这将流通股从 2015 年的约 250 亿股减少到 2025 年的约 148.4 亿股，仅回购一项就使 EPS 每年提高约 2.62%。对于投资者来说，这意味着每股剩余股份代表了 Apple 利润的更大份额，有助于推动股价长期上涨。

**How to find share repurchase amount in MooMoo App:**
Select a company——公司——现金流量表——普通股发行/回购的净额

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-26.png>)

### 2.4.4 并购与收购 (Mergers & Acquisitions, M&A)

**English:**
*   **What Are Mergers & Acquisitions?**
    Mergers happen when two companies combine into one (often as "equals," though one usually dominates). Acquisitions are when one company buys another, and the target becomes part of the buyer. M\&A deals can be friendly (agreed by both) or hostile (unwanted takeover).

*   **Key Things to Know:**
    *   **Types**: Horizontal (same industry, e.g., competitors merging), vertical (supply chain, e.g., buyer acquires supplier), or conglomerate (unrelated businesses).
    *   **How It Works**: Deals involve cash, stock swaps, or both. They require board approval, shareholder votes, and regulatory review (e.g., antitrust checks by the FTC or DOJ).
    *   **Why Companies Do M\&A**: To grow faster, enter new markets, gain technology/assets, cut costs through synergies, or eliminate competition.
    *   **Impact on Investors**: Can boost stock prices if successful (e.g., more market power), but risks include integration failures, debt from financing, or culture clashes leading to value destruction. Target shareholders often get a premium (higher price) for their shares.
    *   **Announcement Effects**: Stocks of the target usually rise; the buyer's may fall short-term due to costs.

**中文:**
*   **什么是并购与收购？**
    合并发生在两家公司合并为一家时（通常是“对等”，尽管一家通常占主导地位）。收购是一家公司收购另一家公司，目标公司成为买方的一部分。并购交易可以是友好的（双方同意）或敌对的（不受欢迎的接管）。

*   **你需要知道的关键事项：**
    *   **类型**: 横向（同行业，如竞争对手合并）、纵向（供应链，如买方收购供应商）或混合（不相关的业务）。
    *   **运作方式**: 交易涉及现金、股票互换或两者兼有。它们需要董事会批准、股东投票和监管审查（例如 FTC 或 DOJ 的反垄断检查）。
    *   **公司为什么进行并购**: 为了更快增长、进入新市场、获得技术/资产、通过协同效应降低成本或消除竞争。
    *   **对投资者的影响**: 如果成功（例如更大的市场力量），可以推高股价，但风险包括整合失败、融资债务或导致价值破坏的文化冲突。目标公司股东通常会为其股份获得溢价（更高的价格）。
    *   **公告效应**: 目标公司的股票通常会上涨；买方的股票可能会因成本短期下跌。

**Example: Microsoft Corporation Acquires Activision Blizzard (2023)**
*   **English**: In 2023, Microsoft Corporation (MSFT) acquired Activision Blizzard Inc. (ATVI), a leading video game publisher known for franchises like Call of Duty, Warcraft, and Candy Crush, in an all-cash deal valued at $68.7 billion (ultimately closing at $75.4 billion). The deal, announced on January 18, 2022, offered $95 per share, a 45% premium over ATVI's closing price of about $65 the day before. This strategic acquisition aimed to expand Microsoft's gaming ecosystem, including Xbox and Game Pass, by adding popular titles and strengthening its position against competitors like Sony and Tencent. The deal faced extensive regulatory scrutiny from bodies like the FTC, CMA (UK), and EU over antitrust concerns but was approved after concessions, closing on October 13, 2023.
*   **中文**: 2023 年，微软公司 (MSFT) 以价值 687 亿美元（最终成交价 754 亿美元）的全现金交易收购了动视暴雪 (ATVI)，这是一家领先的视频游戏发行商，以《使命召唤》、《魔兽争霸》和《糖果粉碎传奇》等特许经营权而闻名。该交易于 2022 年 1 月 18 日宣布，每股报价 95 美元，比 ATVI 前一天约 65 美元的收盘价溢价 45%。这项战略收购旨在通过增加热门游戏并加强其对抗索尼和腾讯等竞争对手的地位，来扩展微软的游戏生态系统，包括 Xbox 和 Game Pass。该交易面临 FTC、CMA（英国）和欧盟等机构因反垄断担忧而进行的广泛监管审查，但在做出让步后获得批准，于 2023 年 10 月 13 日完成。

**Stock Price Performance During the Process / 过程中股价表现:**
*   **Pre-Announcement (January 17, 2022)**: ATVI closed at around $65 per share. MSFT was trading at about $310.
*   **Announcement Day (January 18, 2022)**: ATVI's stock surged nearly 40% in pre-market trading, opening around $90 and closing at $82.31 (a 26% gain but still 13% below the $95 offer, reflecting regulatory uncertainty). MSFT's stock dipped about 2.4%, closing lower due to the high cost and potential risks.
*   **During Regulatory Review (2022-2023)**: ATVI's price fluctuated between $70 and $85, often trading at a discount to $95 amid antitrust challenges and delays. Positive news (e.g., approvals in some countries) caused spikes, while setbacks led to dips. MSFT's stock remained relatively stable but faced pressure from broader market trends and deal-related costs.
*   **Post-Approval and Close (October 2023)**: As approvals came in, ATVI approached $94.42 by the closing date, nearly matching the offer price. ATVI shares were then delisted. MSFT's stock recovered and grew post-close, benefiting from integrated gaming revenues, which boosted its gaming division by nearly 50% in subsequent quarters.

## 2.5 股权的常见指标 (Common Metrics for Equity)

### 2.5.1 股票价格与市值 (Share price and market capitalization)

**English:**
*   **What is Stock Price?**
    The stock price is the current price at which a single share of a company's stock can be bought or sold on the stock market. It's like the price tag on an item in a store—it changes based on supply and demand from investors.
    *   **Why does it matter?** The stock price reflects what investors think the company is worth right now. It can go up if the company is doing well (e.g., strong earnings or new products) or down if there are concerns (e.g., economic slowdowns or competition).

*   **What is Market Capitalization?**
    Market capitalization, or market cap, is the total value of all a company's outstanding shares of stock. It gives a bigger picture of how much the entire company is worth in the eyes of the market.
    *   **Why does it matter?** Market cap helps classify companies: small-cap (under $2 billion), mid-cap ($2-10 billion), large-cap (over $10 billion), and mega-cap (over $200 billion). It's useful for comparing companies and understanding their scale.

**中文:**
*   **什么是股票价格？**
    股票价格是目前在股票市场上买卖公司一股股票的价格。就像商店里商品的价格标签——它根据投资者的供需而变化。
    *   **为什么重要？** 股价反映了投资者认为公司目前的价值。如果公司表现良好（例如强劲的收益或新产品），股价可能会上涨；如果有担忧（例如经济放缓或竞争），股价可能会下跌。

*   **什么是市值？**
    市值是公司所有流通股的总价值。它提供了市场眼中整个公司价值的更大图景。
    *   **为什么重要？** 市值有助于对公司进行分类：小盘股（低于 20 亿美元）、中盘股（20-100 亿美元）、大盘股（超过 100 亿美元）和超大盘股（超过 2000 亿美元）。它对于比较公司和了解其规模很有用。

**How to find share price and market capitalization information in MooMoo App:**
![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-25.png>)

*   **How to Calculate Market Capitalization / 如何计算市值**
    The formula is straightforward: **Market Cap = Current Stock Price × Number of Outstanding Shares**
    公式很简单：**市值 = 当前股价 × 流通股数量**
    *   **Outstanding shares**: This is the total number of shares issued by the company that are held by investors (excluding treasury shares held by the company itself). You can find this in a company's financial reports or on stock data websites.
    *   **Simple analogy**: If a company has 10 shares outstanding and each sells for $5, the whole stand is worth $50 (5 × 10).

*   **Examples**: Let's use two popular US stocks: Apple Inc. (ticker: AAPL) and Tesla Inc. (ticker: TSLA). These examples use actual data as of January 13, 2026, to show the concepts in action.

    *   **Example 1: Apple Inc. (AAPL)**
        *   **Stock Price**: $261.05 per share.
        *   **Outstanding Shares**: Approximately 14.82 billion shares.
        *   **Market Cap Calculation**: $261.05 × 14,820,000,000 ≈ $3.857 trillion.

    *   **Example 2: Tesla Inc. (TSLA)**
        *   **Stock Price**: $447.20 per share.
        *   **Outstanding Shares**: Approximately 3.33 billion shares.
        *   **Market Cap Calculation**: $447.20 × 3,330,000,000 ≈ $1.487 trillion.

### 2.5.2 每股收益与股息收益率 (Earnings Per Share and Dividend Yield)

**English:**
*   **What is Earnings Per Share (EPS)**: EPS measures how much of a company's net profit is allocated to each outstanding share of its stock.
    *   **Why does it matter?** A higher EPS generally indicates that the company is generating more profit per share, which can make the stock more attractive to investors and potentially support a higher stock price. Also, investors can use EPS to derive other valuation metrics, such as price to earnings ratio.
    *   **Types of EPS**: Basic EPS uses the current number of shares, while diluted EPS accounts for potential additional shares from options, convertibles, or other securities (making it more conservative). We'll focus on diluted EPS here.

**中文:**
*   **什么是每股收益 (EPS)**: EPS 衡量分配给每股流通股的公司净利润有多少。
    *   **为什么重要？** 较高的 EPS 通常表明公司每股产生的利润更多，这可以使股票对投资者更具吸引力，并可能支持更高的股价。此外，投资者可以使用 EPS 得出其他估值指标，如市盈率。
    *   **EPS 类型**: 基本 EPS 使用当前的股份数量，而稀释 EPS 考虑了来自期权、可转换债券或其他证券的潜在额外股份（使其更保守）。我们这里重点关注稀释 EPS。

**How to find EPS in MooMoo App:**
Select a company——公司——财务指标——每股收益
![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-24.png>)

**English:**
*   **What is Dividend Yield?**
    Dividend Yield represents the annual dividend payment as a percentage of the current stock price. It indicates the cash return an investor can expect from holding the stock, similar to an interest rate.
    *   **Why does it matter?** It helps income-focused investors evaluate the return from dividends relative to the stock's price. A higher yield might appeal for steady income, but extremely high yields could signal underlying risks, like an unsustainable payout or a falling stock price.
    *   **Dividends explained**: Dividends are distributions of a portion of the company's profits to shareholders, typically paid quarterly. Not all companies pay dividends; many growth-oriented firms reinvest earnings back into the business instead.

**中文:**
*   **什么是股息收益率？**
    股息收益率代表年度股息支付占当前股价的百分比。它表明投资者持有该股票可以预期的现金回报，类似于利率。
    *   **为什么重要？** 它帮助注重收入的投资者评估相对于股价的股息回报。较高的收益率可能因稳定的收入而具有吸引力，但极高的收益率可能预示着潜在风险，如不可持续的支付或股价下跌。
    *   **股息解释**: 股息是将部分公司利润分配给股东，通常按季度支付。并非所有公司都支付股息；许多以增长为导向的公司将收益再投资于业务中。

**How to find dividend yield in MooMoo App:**
Select a company——公司——财务指标——分红派息
![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-23.png>)

**English:**
*   **How to Calculate EPS**
    The formula for diluted EPS is: **EPS = (Net Income - Preferred Dividends) ÷ Average Diluted Shares Outstanding**
    *   **Net Income**: The company's total profit after expenses, taxes, and interest (often for the trailing twelve months, or TTM, for a recent snapshot). "Net Income Applicable to Common Shares" is used, subtracting any preferred dividends.
    *   **Average Diluted Shares Outstanding**: The weighted average number of shares during the period, including potential dilutive securities.
    *   **Simple analogy**: If a bakery earns $100 in profit (after preferred claims) and has 10 shares, each share gets $10 in earnings (EPS = 100 ÷ 10).
    *   **Important note**: Companies report this in financial statements; the calculation accounts for adjustments, so slight discrepancies may occur when verifying manually.

*   **How to Calculate Dividend Yield**
    The formula is:
    **Dividend Yield (TTM) = (Past 12 Month's total Dividend per Share ÷ Current Stock Price) × 100%**
    **Dividend Yield (Forward) = (Expected next year's total Dividend per Share ÷ Current Stock Price) × 100%**
    *   **Annual Dividend per Share (Forward)**: The total dividends expected or paid per share over a year (the forward estimate for the next 12 months).
    *   **Annual Dividend per Share (TTM)**: The total dividends expected or paid per share over a year (total dividend for the past 12 months).
    *   **Current Stock Price**: The most recent trading price.
    *   **Simple analogy**: If a stock is priced at $100 and pays $4 in annual dividends, the yield is 4%—meaning a 4% return from dividends alone.

**中文:**
*   **如何计算 EPS**
    稀释 EPS 的公式为：**EPS = (净利润 - 优先股股息) ÷ 平均稀释流通股**
    *   **净利润**: 扣除费用、税收和利息后的公司总利润（通常为过去十二个月，或 TTM，以获取最新快照）。使用“归属于普通股的净利润”，减去任何优先股股息。
    *   **平均稀释流通股**: 期间的加权平均股份数量，包括潜在的稀释证券。
    *   **简单类比**: 如果一家面包店赚了 100 美元利润（扣除优先索赔后）并有 10 股，每股获得 10 美元收益（EPS = 100 ÷ 10）。
    *   **重要提示**: 公司在财务报表中报告此数据；计算考虑了调整，因此在手动验证时可能会出现细微差异。

*   **如何计算股息收益率**
    公式为：
    **股息收益率 (TTM) = (过去 12 个月的每股总股息 ÷ 当前股价) × 100%**
    **股息收益率 (Forward) = (预期明年的每股总股息 ÷ 当前股价) × 100%**
    *   **每股年度股息 (Forward)**: 一年内预期或支付的每股总股息（未来 12 个月的远期估计）。
    *   **每股年度股息 (TTM)**: 一年内预期或支付的每股总股息（过去 12 个月的总股息）。
    *   **当前股价**: 最近的交易价格。
    *   **简单类比**: 如果一只股票价格为 100 美元并支付 4 美元的年度股息，则收益率为 4%——意味着仅股息就有 4% 的回报。

**Examples**: Let's examine two major US stocks: Apple Inc. (AAPL) and Microsoft Corporation (MSFT). Both demonstrate strong per-share profitability and pay dividends. Data is as of January 14, 2026.

*   **Example 1: Apple Inc. (AAPL)**
    *   **Net Income (TTM)**: $112.01 billion.
    *   **Diluted Shares Outstanding (TTM)**: Approximately 14.78 billion.
    *   **Annual Dividend per Share (Forward)**: $1.04.
    *   **Stock Price**: $261.05.
    *   **Dividend Yield (Forward)**: 0.40%. (Calculated as (1.04 ÷ 261.05) × 100%.)

*   **Example 2: Microsoft Corporation (MSFT)**
    *   **Net Income (TTM)**: $104.91 billion.
    *   **Diluted Shares Outstanding (TTM)**: Approximately 7.43 billion.
    *   **Annual Dividend per Share (Forward)**: $3.64.
### 2.5.3 **净资产收益率（ROE）：衡量公司盈利能力的指标 ROE (Return on Equity): Measure of Company Profitability**

Simplified Definition: "How much profit a company makes for every $1 of shareholder equity" (Formula: ROE = Net Income( t)/ ((Shareholders’ Equity(t-1) +Shareholders’ Equity(t+1))/2). Higher ROE = more efficient at turning equity into profits.

简化定义：“公司每 1 美元股东权益能产生多少利润”（公式：ROE = 净利润(t) / ((股东权益(t-1) + 股东权益(t+1))/2)）。ROE 越高 = 将权益转化为利润的效率越高。

2025 财年，苹果公司的 ROE 为 171.42%

Calculation Process:
计算过程：

Apple ROE(FY2025) = Net income (FY2025) / ((Shareholder's equity (FY2024) + Shareholder's equity (FY2025))/2) = 1120.1/ ((737.33+569.2)/2) =171%
苹果 ROE(2025财年) = 净利润 (2025财年) / ((股东权益 (2024财年) + 股东权益 (2025财年))/2) = 1120.1/ ((737.33+569.2)/2) = 171%

### 2.5.4 **价格乘数（市盈率和市净率）Price Multiples (P/E Ratio and P/B Ratio)**

Price multiples = compare a stock’s price to a key financial metric (e.g., earnings, net assets) to judge if it’s cheap or expensive. It’s like buying groceries: you compare the "price per pound" of apples—multiples let you compare "price per unit of profit/assets" across stocks. No complex formulas, just "price ÷ key metric."

价格乘数 = 将股票价格与关键财务指标（如盈利、净资产）进行比较，以判断其便宜还是昂贵。这就像买菜：你比较苹果的“每磅价格”——乘数让你比较不同股票的“每单位利润/资产的价格”。没有复杂的公式，就是“价格 ÷ 关键指标”。

| **Multiple Type**<br>**乘数类型** | **Explanation & Simple Formula**<br>**解释与简单公式** | **Step-by-Step U.S. Case (2024)**<br>**美国案例分步解析 (2024)** | **Interpretation**<br>**解读** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **P/E Ratio (Price/Earnings)**<br>**市盈率 (P/E)** | Meaning: **How much you pay for $1 of the company’s annual earnings.**<br>含义：**你为公司每 1 美元的年度盈利支付了多少钱。**<br><br>**Formula: P/E = Stock price per share ÷ Earnings per share (EPS).**<br>**公式：市盈率 = 每股股价 ÷ 每股收益 (EPS)。**<br><br>**"Forward P/E"** (based on expected next-year earnings) is more commonly used than **"trailing P/E"** (past earnings) and **static P.E** (using most recent fiscal year's earnings).<br>**“远期市盈率”**（基于预期的明年盈利）比**“滚动市盈率”**（过去盈利）和**静态市盈率**（使用最近一个财年的盈利）更常用。<br><br>PE (TTM) reflects how much investors are willing to pay for each unit of a company’s earnings generated over the most recent consecutive twelve months.<br>PE (TTM) 反映了投资者愿意为公司在过去连续 12 个月内产生的每单位盈利支付多少钱。<br><br>**Formula**：PE (TTM) = Current Share Price / Trailing Twelve Months Earnings Per Share (EPS)<br>**公式**：PE (TTM) = 当前股价 / 过去 12 个月每股收益 (EPS) | Case1: Tesla (TSLA)<br>案例 1：特斯拉 (TSLA)<br><br>Get data: Tesla’s Earnings per Share (Diluted) for the trailing twelve months (TTM) ended in Sep. 2025 was $1.50 (See data from Moomoo below). Today (2026.01.05)'s share price is 453.5. The PE(TTM) is 453.5/1.5=302.3X<br>获取数据：截至 2025 年 9 月，特斯拉过去 12 个月 (TTM) 的稀释每股收益为 1.50 美元（见下方 Moomoo 数据）。今天 (2026.01.05) 的股价为 453.5。PE(TTM) 为 453.5/1.5 = 302.3 倍 | You pay $302.3 for every $1 of Tesla’s trailing twelve months, which is much higher than the market average. This means investors expect Tesla to grow much faster than average (so they’re willing to pay a premium).<br>你为特斯拉过去 12 个月的每 1 美元盈利支付了 302.3 美元，这远高于市场平均水平。这意味着投资者预期特斯拉的增长速度将远超平均水平（因此他们愿意支付溢价）。 |

This reflects investors think Bank of America has better asset quality (e.g., fewer bad loans) than most banks.<br>你为美国银行每 1 美元的净资产支付了 1.5 美元，略高于行业平均水平。这反映出投资者认为美国银行的资产质量（例如，坏账较少）优于大多数银行。 |
