#### **Something Practical about PE ratio and PB ratio:**
#### **关于 PE 和 PB 比率的实战知识：**

* **Why Some Companies Have High P/E Ratios While Others Have Low Ones:**
* **为什么有些公司的市盈率很高，而有些很低：**

The price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio measures a company’s current share price relative to its earnings per share (EPS), reflecting how much investors are willing to pay for each dollar of the company’s earnings. The divergence between high and low P/E ratios stems from multiple factors related to a company’s performance, industry dynamics, and market expectations. Here is a detailed breakdown:
市盈率 (P/E) 衡量公司当前股价与其每股收益 (EPS) 的关系，反映了投资者愿意为公司每一美元的盈利支付多少钱。高市盈率和低市盈率的差异源于与公司业绩、行业动态和市场预期相关的多种因素。以下是详细分类：

* **Reasons for High P/E Ratios**
* **高市盈率的原因**

  * **Strong Growth Expectations**
  * **强劲的增长预期**

  This is the most common driver of high P/E ratios. Investors are willing to pay a premium for companies expected to deliver above-average earnings growth in the future. For example, startups or firms in emerging sectors (e.g., artificial intelligence, renewable energy tech) often have minimal current earnings but promising pipelines of products or services. The market bets on their ability to scale rapidly and generate substantial profits later, pushing up share prices relative to current EPS.
  这是高市盈率最常见的驱动因素。投资者愿意为预期未来盈利增长高于平均水平的公司支付溢价。例如，初创企业或新兴行业（如人工智能、可再生能源技术）的公司通常当前盈利极少，但拥有前景广阔的产品或服务管线。市场押注它们能够快速扩张并在后期产生巨额利润，从而推高了相对于当前 EPS 的股价。

  * **Favorable Industry Traits**
  * **有利的行业特征**

  Companies in high-growth, asset-light industries (e.g., software, digital services) typically have higher P/E ratios than those in capital-intensive, mature sectors. These industries usually feature high profit margins, low production costs, and scalable business models, which support sustained earnings growth. In contrast, capital-heavy industries face higher operational costs and slower expansion potential, limiting their P/E upside.
  处于高增长、轻资产行业（如软件、数字服务）的公司通常比资本密集型、成熟行业的公司拥有更高的市盈率。这些行业通常具有高利润率、低生产成本和可扩展的商业模式，支持持续的盈利增长。相比之下，重资产行业面临更高的运营成本和较慢的扩张潜力，限制了其市盈率的上升空间。

  * **Low Dividend Payout Policies**
  * **低股息支付政策**

  Firms that reinvest most of their earnings into research and development (R\&D), expansion, or acquisitions instead of paying dividends tend to have higher P/E ratios. Investors value this reinvestment strategy because it signals the company’s focus on long-term growth, which is expected to drive future earnings and share price appreciation.
  将大部分盈利再投资于研发 (R\&D)、扩张或收购而不是支付股息的公司，往往具有更高的市盈率。投资者看重这种再投资策略，因为它表明公司专注于长期增长，预计这将推动未来的盈利和股价上涨。

  * **Positive Market Sentiment and Speculation**
  * **积极的市场情绪和投机**

  In bull markets or for companies at the center of market trends (e.g., a social media platform during a user boom), investor enthusiasm and speculative buying can inflate share prices beyond what current earnings justify. This hype-driven demand leads to elevated P/E ratios, even if the company’s fundamentals do not fully support the valuation.
  在牛市中或处于市场趋势中心的公司（例如用户激增期间的社交媒体平台），投资者的热情和投机性买入可能会将股价推高至当前盈利无法证明的水平。这种炒作驱动的需求导致市盈率升高，即使公司的基本面并不完全支持该估值。

* **Reasons for Low P/E Ratios:**
* **低市盈率的原因：**

  * **Mature or Declining Industry Position:**
  * **成熟或衰退的行业地位：**

  Companies in mature, slow-growth industries (e.g., utilities, traditional manufacturing) often have low P/E ratios. Their earnings are stable but lack significant growth potential, as the market is saturated, and competition is intense. Investors have limited expectations for future earnings growth, so they are unwilling to pay a high premium for current earnings.
  处于成熟、低增长行业（如公用事业、传统制造业）的公司通常市盈率较低。它们的盈利稳定，但缺乏显著的增长潜力，因为市场已饱和且竞争激烈。投资者对未来盈利增长的预期有限，因此不愿为当前盈利支付高溢价。

  * **Cyclical Business Nature:**
  * **周期性业务性质：**

  Cyclical companies (e.g., steel, automotive, construction) experience earnings fluctuations tied to economic cycles. During economic booms, their earnings surge, which lowers the P/E ratio (since EPS rises while share prices may not keep pace immediately). Conversely, in downturns, earnings drop, but P/E ratios may not rise sharply if investors anticipate a prolonged slump.
  周期性公司（如钢铁、汽车、建筑）的盈利波动与经济周期挂钩。在经济繁荣时期，它们的盈利激增，从而降低了市盈率（因为 EPS 上升而股价可能不会立即跟上）。相反，在经济低迷时期，盈利下降，但如果投资者预计经济将长期低迷，市盈率可能不会大幅上升。

  * **Perceived Risks or Operational Challenges**
  * **感知到的风险或运营挑战**

  A low P/E ratio can reflect investor concerns about a company’s prospects, such as poor management decisions, high debt levels, legal issues, or declining market share. These risks make investors cautious, leading them to demand a lower share price relative to earnings, resulting in a compressed P/E ratio.
  低市盈率可能反映了投资者对公司前景的担忧，例如糟糕的管理决策、高债务水平、法律问题或市场份额下降。这些风险使投资者变得谨慎，导致他们要求相对于盈利更低的股价，从而压缩了市盈率。

  * **High Dividend Payouts:**
  * **高股息支付：**

  Some companies, especially in mature sectors like utilities or consumer staples, distribute a large portion of their earnings as dividends. While dividends attract income-focused investors, the lack of reinvestment for growth means the company’s earnings potential is limited. Thus, the market assigns a lower P/E ratio, as investors prioritize steady dividend income over capital gains.
  一些公司，特别是公用事业或消费必需品等成熟行业的公司，将大部分盈利作为股息分发。虽然股息吸引了注重收入的投资者，但缺乏用于增长的再投资意味着公司的盈利潜力有限。因此，市场给予较低的市盈率，因为投资者优先考虑稳定的股息收入而非资本增值。

> **Case: Nvidia VS AT\&T**
> **案例：英伟达 (Nvidia) VS AT\&T**
>
> Nvidia has a much higher growth rate than AT\&T, and its PE ratio is much higher as well. The most important reason that makes difference in PE ratio is the company's growth potential.
> 英伟达的增长率远高于 AT\&T，其市盈率也高得多。造成市盈率差异的最重要原因是公司的增长潜力。

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-39.png>)

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-38.png>)

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-37.png>)

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-35.png>)

* **Why Some Companies Have High P/B Ratios While Others Have Low Ones:**
* **为什么有些公司的市净率很高，而有些很低：**

The price-to-book (P/B) ratio compares a company’s market capitalization to its book value of equity (total assets minus total liabilities, divided by the number of outstanding shares). It reflects how much investors are willing to pay for each dollar of a company’s net assets. The gap between high and low P/B ratios mainly depends on the quality of a company’s assets, its profitability, and market expectations for future returns. Here is a detailed explanation:
市净率 (P/B) 将公司的市值与其权益账面价值（总资产减去总负债，除以流通股数）进行比较。它反映了投资者愿意为公司的每一美元净资产支付多少钱。高市净率和低市净率之间的差距主要取决于公司的资产质量、盈利能力和市场对未来回报的预期。以下是详细解释：

* **Reasons for High P/B Ratios:**
* **高市净率的原因：**

  * **Strong Asset Profitability (High ROE)**
  * **强大的资产盈利能力（高 ROE）**

  The most critical driver of a high P/B ratio is a company’s ability to generate substantial profits from its net assets, measured by return on equity (ROE). Companies with high ROE—such as those in software, digital services, or luxury goods—often operate with asset-light business models. Their book value may be relatively low (e.g., minimal physical assets like factories), but they can leverage intangible resources (e.g., software algorithms, brand loyalty) to earn far above-average returns. Investors are willing to pay a premium for these efficient assets, pushing up the P/B ratio.
  高市净率最关键的驱动因素是公司从净资产中产生巨额利润的能力，以净资产收益率 (ROE) 衡量。高 ROE 的公司——如软件、数字服务或奢侈品行业的公司——通常以轻资产商业模式运营。它们的账面价值可能相对较低（例如，极少的工厂等实物资产），但它们可以利用无形资源（例如，软件算法、品牌忠诚度）获得远高于平均水平的回报。投资者愿意为这些高效资产支付溢价，从而推高了市净率。

  * **Undervalued Intangible Assets**
  * **被低估的无形资产**

  Book value is based on historical cost accounting, which often understates the value of intangible assets that are not fully recorded on the balance sheet. For example: Tech firms with valuable patents, proprietary technology, or strong customer bases. Luxury brands with high brand recognition and customer loyalty.
  账面价值基于历史成本会计，这往往低估了未完全记录在资产负债表上的无形资产的价值。例如：拥有有价值专利、专有技术或强大客户群的科技公司。拥有高品牌知名度和客户忠诚度的奢侈品牌。

  These intangibles are critical to revenue and profit growth but may not be reflected in the book value. The market recognizes their true worth, leading to a higher market price relative to the stated book value, hence a high P/B ratio.
  这些无形资产对收入和利润增长至关重要，但可能不会反映在账面价值中。市场认可它们的真实价值，导致相对于账面价值的市场价格较高，因此市净率较高。

  * **Favorable Industry Prospects and Competitive Moats**
  * **有利的行业前景和竞争护城河**

  Companies in high-growth industries (e.g., artificial intelligence, renewable energy) or those with strong competitive moats (e.g., network effects, regulatory barriers) tend to have high P/B ratios. Investors anticipate that these companies will continue to grow and maintain high profitability, so they are willing to pay more for their net assets. For instance, a leading cloud computing firm may have a high P/B ratio because its scalable platform and market dominance create sustainable value beyond its recorded book value.
  处于高增长行业（如人工智能、可再生能源）或拥有强大竞争护城河（如网络效应、监管壁垒）的公司往往具有较高的市净率。投资者预计这些公司将继续增长并保持高盈利能力，因此愿意为其净资产支付更多费用。例如，一家领先的云计算公司可能拥有高市净率，因为其可扩展的平台和市场主导地位创造了超出其记录账面价值的可持续价值。

  * **Positive Market Sentiment**
  * **积极的市场情绪**

  In bull markets or during periods of sector-specific hype, investor enthusiasm can inflate share prices independently of book value. Speculative buying driven by optimism about future growth can push P/B ratios to elevated levels, even if a company’s current asset profitability is moderate.
  在牛市或特定行业炒作期间，投资者的热情可能会独立于账面价值推高股价。由对未来增长的乐观情绪驱动的投机性买入可以将市净率推高至高位，即使公司当前的资产盈利能力一般。

* **Reasons for Low P/B Ratios**
* **低市净率的原因**

  * **Weak Asset Profitability (Low ROE)**
  * **资产盈利能力弱（低 ROE）**

  Companies with low ROE struggle to generate meaningful profits from their net assets, which leads to low P/B ratios. This is common in capital-intensive, mature industries like traditional manufacturing, steel production, or coal mining. These firms require massive investments in physical assets (factories, machinery, mines), which inflate their book value. However, due to overcapacity, low profit margins, or slow demand growth, their ROE remains low. Investors see little value in these underperforming assets, so they are unwilling to pay a premium over book value.
  低 ROE 的公司难以从净资产中产生有意义的利润，这导致低市净率。这在传统制造业、钢铁生产或煤炭开采等资本密集型、成熟行业中很常见。这些公司需要对实物资产（工厂、机械、矿山）进行巨额投资，这会使账面价值膨胀。然而，由于产能过剩、低利润率或需求增长缓慢，它们的 ROE 仍然很低。投资者认为这些表现不佳的资产价值不大，因此不愿支付高于账面价值的溢价。

  * **Poor Asset Quality or Depreciating Assets**
  * **资产质量差或资产贬值**

  A low P/B ratio can signal that a company’s book value is inflated by low-quality or depreciating assets. For example: A manufacturing company with outdated machinery that has little resale value. A retail firm with excess inventory or unoccupied store locations that are losing value. In such cases, the book value on the balance sheet does not reflect the actual market value of the assets. Investors discount the stock price to account for this gap, resulting in a low P/B ratio.
  低市净率可能表明公司的账面价值因低质量或贬值的资产而虚高。例如：一家拥有转售价值极低的过时机械的制造公司。一家拥有过剩库存或空置店面且正在贬值的零售公司。在这种情况下，资产负债表上的账面价值并不能反映资产的实际市场价值。投资者会对股价进行折价以弥补这一差距，从而导致低市净率。

  * **Cyclical Industry Downturns**
  * **周期性行业低迷**

  Cyclical companies (e.g., automotive, construction, commodities) experience sharp swings in earnings tied to economic cycles. During a downturn, demand for their products collapses, leading to falling revenues and asset write-downs. Even if book value remains stable in the short term, investors anticipate further declines in asset value and profitability, so they sell the stock, pushing the P/B ratio down. In some cases, P/B ratios may drop below 1, meaning the market values the company at less than its stated net assets.
  周期性公司（如汽车、建筑、大宗商品）的盈利随经济周期大幅波动。在经济低迷时期，对其产品的需求崩溃，导致收入下降和资产减记。即使账面价值在短期内保持稳定，投资者也预计资产价值和盈利能力将进一步下降，因此他们抛售股票，压低市净率。在某些情况下，市净率可能降至 1 以下，这意味着市场对公司的估值低于其声称的净资产。

  * **High Financial Risk or Operational Distress**
  * **高财务风险或运营困境**

  Companies burdened with high debt, legal issues, or declining market share often have low P/B ratios. High debt increases financial risk and reduces the value of equity (since liabilities are subtracted from assets to calculate book value). Operational distress—such as shrinking sales or management missteps—erodes investor confidence, leading to a lower stock price relative to book value.
  背负高额债务、法律问题或市场份额下降的公司通常市净率较低。高债务增加了财务风险并降低了权益价值（因为在计算账面价值时要从资产中减去负债）。运营困境——如销售萎缩或管理失误——会侵蚀投资者信心，导致股价相对于账面价值较低。

  In summary, the P/B ratio is a gauge of asset quality and profitability: high P/B ratios indicate that investors believe a company’s assets can generate strong future returns, while low P/B ratios reflect weak asset profitability, poor asset quality, or market pessimism.
  总之，市净率是衡量资产质量和盈利能力的指标：高市净率表明投资者相信公司的资产可以产生强劲的未来回报，而低市净率反映了资产盈利能力弱、资产质量差或市场悲观。

> **Case: The Relationship Between PB Ratio and ROE in Chinese Bank Sector (Data Source: iFind, Date: 2026.01.06)**
> **案例：中国银行业 PB 比率与 ROE 的关系（数据来源：iFind，日期：2026.01.06）**

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-36.png>)

**Explanation:** Banks with higher ROE generally have higher PB ratio, there is a significant positive relationship between PB ratio and ROE.
**解释：** ROE 较高的银行通常具有较高的 PB 比率，PB 比率与 ROE 之间存在显著的正相关关系。

## 2.6 以下例题（**Practice Questions**）

| **1.What is the primary market in stock trading?**<br />**1. 股票交易中的一级市场是什么？**<br />A) Where investors trade existing stocks among themselves<br />A) 投资者之间交易现有股票的地方<br />B) Where companies issue new stocks for the first time, like an IPO<br />B) 公司首次发行新股的地方，如 IPO<br />C) Where only preferred stocks are sold<br />C) 只出售优先股的地方 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** The primary market is for new issuances, such as Airbnb's 2020 IPO, where companies raise capital by selling fresh shares directly to investors.<br />**解释：** 一级市场用于新发行，例如 Airbnb 2020 年的 IPO，公司通过直接向投资者出售新股来筹集资金。 |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **2.In the secondary market, when you buy a stock like Apple, who are you buying it from?**<br />**2. 在二级市场，当你购买像苹果这样的股票时，你是从谁那里买的？**<br />A) Directly from Apple<br />A) 直接从苹果公司<br />B) From another investor<br />B) 从另一位投资者<br />C) From a foreign bank<br />C) 从外国银行 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** The secondary market involves trading existing shares between investors, not from the company itself, similar to a used car sale.<br />**解释：** 二级市场涉及投资者之间现有股份的交易，而不是来自公司本身，类似于二手车销售。 |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **3.Which type of stock typically gives voting rights on company decisions?**<br />**3. 哪种类型的股票通常拥有公司决策的投票权？**<br />A) Preferred shares<br />A) 优先股<br />B) Common shares<br />B) 普通股<br />C) ADRs<br />C) ADRs | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** Common shares allow voting, while preferred shares have limited or no voting rights.<br />**解释：** 普通股允许投票，而优先股的投票权有限或没有。 |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |

| **4.In case of company liquidation, who gets paid before common shareholders?**<br />**4. 在公司清算的情况下，谁比普通股股东先获得赔偿？**<br />A) Common shareholders<br />A) 普通股股东<br />B) Debt holders and preferred shareholders<br />B) 债权人和优先股股东<br />C) Only foreign investors<br />C) 仅外国投资者 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation: **Preferred shares have higher liquidation priority, getting paid after debt holders but before common shareholders.<br />**解释：** 优先股具有较高的清算优先级，在债权人之后但在普通股股东之前获得赔偿。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

|  **5.What is ADR?**<br />**5. 什么是 ADR？**<br />A) A type of preferred stock<br />A) 一种优先股<br />B) A proxy for foreign stocks traded on U.S. exchanges in USD<br />B) 在美国交易所交易的以美元计价的外国股票代理<br />C) A stock split mechanism<br />C) 一种股票拆分机制 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** ADRs, like BABA for Alibaba, allow U.S. investors to buy foreign stocks easily without currency conversion or foreign brokers.<br />**解释：** ADR（如阿里巴巴的 BABA）允许美国投资者轻松购买外国股票，无需货币兑换或外国经纪人。 |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |

| **6.What is a dividend?**<br />**6. 什么是股息？**<br />A) A company buying back its shares<br />A) 公司回购其股份<br />B) Payments from profits to shareholders<br />B) 从利润中支付给股东的款项<br />C) A merger between two companies<br />C) 两家公司之间的合并 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** Dividends are profit distributions, often cash, paid quarterly by mature companies like Apple to reward shareholders.<br />**解释：** 股息是利润分配，通常是现金，由像苹果这样的成熟公司按季度支付以回报股东。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **7.What is the ex-dividend date?**<br />**7. 什么是除息日？**<br />A) The date dividends are paid<br />A) 支付股息的日期<br />B) The cutoff date to qualify for the next dividend<br />B) 获得下一次股息资格的截止日期<br />C) The announcement date of a stock split<br />C) 股票拆分的公告日期 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation: **To receive the dividend, you must own the stock before the ex-dividend date; buying on or after means you miss it.<br />**解释：** 要获得股息，你必须在除息日之前持有股票；在当天或之后购买意味着你将错过它。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **8.What happens in a 2-for-1 stock split?**<br />**8. 在 2 拆 1 的股票拆分中会发生什么？**<br />A) Shares double, price halves, total value stays the same<br />A) 股份翻倍，价格减半，总价值保持不变<br />B) Company pays extra dividends<br />B) 公司支付额外股息<br />C) Shares are bought back<br />C) 股份被回购 | **Answer: A**<br />**答案：A**<br />**Explanation: **Splits make shares more affordable, like Apple's 2020 4-for-1 split, increasing liquidity without changing investment value.<br />**解释：** 拆分使股票更实惠，就像苹果 2020 年的 4 拆 1 拆分一样，增加了流动性而不改变投资价值。 |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **9.What is a stock buyback?**<br />**9. 什么是股票回购？**<br />A) Issuing new shares<br />A) 发行新股<br />B) Company repurchasing its own shares to reduce outstanding count<br />B) 公司回购自己的股份以减少流通股数量<br />C) Paying dividends in stock<br />C) 以股票支付股息 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** Company repurchasing its own shares to reduce outstanding count<br />**解释：** 公司回购自己的股份以减少流通股数量 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **10.What does EPS measure?**<br />**10. EPS 衡量什么？**<br />A) Profit per share<br />A) 每股利润<br />B) Dividend yield<br />B) 股息收益率<br />C) Market cap<br />C) 市值 | **Answer: A**<br />**答案：A**<br />**Explanation: **EPS = (Net Income - Preferred Dividends) / Diluted Shares, indicating profitability per share, like Apple's calculation.<br />**解释：** EPS =（净利润 - 优先股股息）/ 稀释股份，表示每股盈利能力，如苹果的计算。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **11.Why do some companies have high P/B ratios?**<br />**11. 为什么有些公司的 P/B 比率很高？**<br />A) Low asset profitability<br />A) 资产盈利能力低<br />B) Strong ROE and undervalued intangibles<br />B) 强劲的 ROE 和被低估的无形资产<br />C) High dividends only<br />C) 仅高股息 | **Answer: B**<br />**答案：B**<br />**Explanation: **High P/B reflects efficient assets and high ROE, as in tech firms with intangibles like patents, versus low in capital-heavy industries.<br />**解释：** 高 P/B 反映了高效资产和高 ROE，如拥有专利等无形资产的科技公司，而资本密集型行业则较低。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
# 3. 交易所交易基金（ETF）

> 考试小贴士：本章节题目占比为10%左右，基本上以概念的考察为主。
>
> * 要点：杠杆ETF是平台热门产品，需理解他的作用、原理和优缺点。对于常见的ETF和平台热门ETF，需充分其底层标的和具体信息。
>
> * 产品层面：如何在App中查找ETF的相关信息并且进行不同ETF的对比、平台热门ETF有哪些？

**An Exchange-Traded Fund (ETF) is an investment fund that holds a diversified basket of assets, such as stocks, bonds, commodities, or other securities, and trades on stock exchanges like individual stocks.** ETFs are designed to track the performance of a specific index, sector, commodity, or theme, providing investors with broad market exposure without needing to buy each underlying asset individually. Unlike mutual funds, ETFs can be bought and sold throughout the trading day at market prices, offering liquidity and flexibility.

**交易所交易基金 (ETF) 是一种投资基金，持有股票、债券、商品或其他证券等多元化资产篮子，并像个股一样在证券交易所交易。** ETF 旨在追踪特定指数、行业、商品或主题的表现，为投资者提供广泛的市场敞口，而无需单独购买每种标的资产。与共同基金不同，ETF 可以在整个交易日内以市场价格买卖，提供流动性和灵活性。

## 3.1 ETF的主要特征（**Key characteristics**）

* **Pooled Investments**: ETFs pool money from multiple investors to purchase a portfolio of assets.
* **集合投资**：ETF 汇集多位投资者的资金购买资产组合。

* **Exchange-Traded**: Traded on major exchanges (e.g., NYSE, NASDAQ) with real-time pricing.
* **交易所交易**：在主要交易所（如纽交所、纳斯达克）交易，具有实时定价。

* **Passive vs. Active**: Most are passive (index-tracking), but active ETFs, managed by professionals, are growing rapidly.
* **被动与主动**：大多数是被动的（指数追踪），但由专业人士管理的主动型 ETF 正在迅速增长。

## 3.2 美国ETF发展史（History of ETFs in the U.S.）

ETFs originated in the U.S. in 1993 with the launch of the SPDR S\&P 500 ETF Trust (SPY), the first ETF, which tracked the S\&P 500 index. This innovation combined the diversification of mutual funds with the tradability of stocks. The market grew slowly initially but exploded in the 2000s due to regulatory changes and investor demand for low-cost, efficient vehicles.

ETF 起源于 1993 年的美国，当时推出了 SPDR S\&P 500 ETF Trust (SPY)，这是第一只追踪标准普尔 500 指数的 ETF。这一创新结合了共同基金的多元化和股票的可交易性。市场最初增长缓慢，但在 2000 年代由于监管变化和投资者对低成本、高效工具的需求而爆发式增长。

* **Milestones:**
* **里程碑：**

* **2000s**: Introduction of sector, international, and bond ETFs.
* **2000年代**：引入行业、国际和债券 ETF。

* **2010s**: Rise of smart-beta and thematic ETFs; AUM surpassed $1 trillion by 2010.
* **2010年代**：Smart-beta 和主题 ETF 兴起；资产管理规模 (AUM) 在 2010 年超过 1 万亿美元。

* **2020s**: Surge in active ETFs; by 2025, active ETFs represent 54% of all U.S.-listed ETFs, with AUM exceeding $1.17 trillion in mid-2025. Total ETFs launched in 2025: 1,167, a record.
* **2020年代**：主动型 ETF 激增；到 2025 年，主动型 ETF 占所有美国上市 ETF 的 54%，AUM 在 2025 年中期超过 1.17 万亿美元。2025 年推出的 ETF 总数达到创纪录的 1,167 只。

## 3.3 ETF的运作原理（**How ETFs Work**）

ETFs operate through a creation/redemption mechanism involving authorized participants (APs), typically large institutions. APs create new ETF shares by delivering a basket of underlying securities to the ETF provider and redeem shares by exchanging them for the underlying assets. This process keeps the ETF's market price close to its net asset value (NAV), minimizing premiums or discounts.

ETF 通过涉及授权参与者 (AP)（通常是大型机构）的申购/赎回机制运作。AP 通过向 ETF 提供商交付一篮子标的证券来创建新的 ETF 份额，并通过将其交换为标的资产来赎回份额。此过程使 ETF 的市场价格接近其资产净值 (NAV)，从而最大限度地减少溢价或折价。

* **Pricing**: Intraday trading at market prices; NAV calculated daily.
* **定价**：以市场价格进行日内交易；NAV 每日计算。

* **Expenses**: Low expense ratios (average around 0.4% for passive ETFs) due to passive management.
* **费用**：由于被动管理，费用率较低（被动 ETF 平均约为 0.4%）。

* **Tax Efficiency**: In-kind redemptions reduce capital gains distributions compared to mutual funds.
* **税务效率**：与共同基金相比，实物赎回减少了资本收益分配。

ETFs come in various forms to suit different investment strategies.
ETF 有多种形式以适应不同的投资策略。

## 3.5 购买ETF前需要关注的指标（Metrics to watch before buying an ETF）

| **Metric**<br>**指标** | **Description**<br>**描述** | **Why It Matters**<br>**重要原因** | **Example/Threshold**<br>**示例/阈值** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Expense Ratio**<br>**费用率** | Annual fee as a percentage of AUM, covering management and operations.<br>作为 AUM 百分比的年费，涵盖管理和运营。 | Lower ratios preserve returns; average for passive ETFs is around 0.4%, with many below 0.1%.<br>较低的比率可保留回报；被动 ETF 的平均水平约为 0.4%，许多低于 0.1%。 | Aim for <0.2% for broad-market ETFs.<br>对于大盘 ETF，目标 <0.2%。 |
| **Assets Under Management (AUM)**<br>**资产管理规模** | Total value of assets in the ETF.<br>ETF 中的总资产价值。 | Larger AUM (> $1 billion) indicates stability, liquidity, and lower closure risk.<br>较大的 AUM（> 10 亿美元）表明稳定性、流动性和较低的关闭风险。 | Avoid ETFs with AUM < $100 million unless specialized.<br>除非是专业 ETF，否则避免 AUM < 1 亿美元的 ETF。 |
| **Trading Volume and Liquidity**<br>**交易量和流动性** | Average daily shares traded; bid-ask spread measures trading cost.<br>日均交易股数；买卖价差衡量交易成本。 | High volume (>100,000 shares/day) ensures easy entry/exit without price impact; spreads <0.1%.<br>高成交量（>10万股/天）确保轻松进出且不影响价格；价差 <0.1%。 | Check for narrow spreads to minimize slippage.<br>检查窄价差以尽量减少滑点。 |
| **Tracking Error**<br>**跟踪误差** | Deviation from the benchmark index's performance.<br>偏离基准指数表现的程度。 | Low error (<0.5%) shows effective replication; higher indicates inefficiency.<br>低误差（<0.5%）表明有效复制；较高则表明效率低下。 | Compare to index over 1-3 years.<br>与 1-3 年的指数进行比较。 |
| **Holdings and Diversification**<br>**持仓和多元化** | Number and composition of underlying assets.<br>标的资产的数量和构成。 | Ensures broad exposure; review top holdings to avoid concentration (>10% in one stock).<br>确广泛敞口；审查前几大持仓以避免集中（>10% 在一只股票上）。 | Favor ETFs with 50+ holdings for diversification.<br>倾向于拥有 50+ 持仓的 ETF 以实现多元化。 |
| **Tax Efficiency**<br>**税务效率** | Frequency of capital gains distributions.<br>资本收益分配的频率。 | ETFs are generally tax-efficient due to in-kind redemptions; check historical distributions.<br>由于实物赎回，ETF 通常具有税务效率；检查历史分配。 | Prefer those with minimal taxable events.<br>首选应税事件最少的 ETF。 |
| **Performance History**<br>**历史表现** | Returns over 1, 3, 5 years vs. benchmark.<br>1、3、5 年相对于基准的回报。 | Past performance isn't predictive but reveals consistency; include risk-adjusted metrics like Sharpe ratio.<br>过去的表现不能预测未来，但能揭示一致性；包括夏普比率等风险调整指标。 | Use tools like Morningstar for comparisons.<br>使用晨星等工具进行比较。 |
| **Yield and Distributions**<br>**收益率和分配** | Dividend yield or income generated.<br>股息收益率或产生的收入。 | Important for income-focused investors<br>对注重收入的投资者很重要 | Match to needs, e.g., >3% for dividend ETFs.<br>匹配需求，例如股息 ETF >3%。 |
| **Issuer Reputation**<br>**发行人声誉** | Track record of the provider (e.g., Vanguard, BlackRock).<br>提供商的过往记录（如 Vanguard, BlackRock）。 | Established issuers offer better oversight and innovation; review for regulatory compliance.<br>老牌发行人提供更好的监督和创新；审查监管合规性。 | Stick to top issuers with >10 years in ETFs.<br>坚持选择拥有 >10 年 ETF 经验的顶级发行人。 |

## 3.6 投资ETF的全面分析（**Advantages/disadvantages of Investing in ETFs**）

| **Advantages:**<br>**优势：**<br>ETFs are popular for beginners due to their simplicity and cost-effectiveness.<br>ETF 因其简单且具有成本效益而受到初学者的欢迎。<br>**Diversification**: Spread risk across many assets with one purchase.<br>**多元化**：通过一次购买将风险分散到许多资产上。<br>**Low Costs**: Expense ratios are often below 0.1% for broad-market ETFs.<br>**低成本**：大盘 ETF 的费用率通常低于 0.1%。<br>**Liquidity**: Easy to buy/sell during market hours.<br>**流动性**：在交易时间内易于买卖。<br>**Transparency**: Holdings disclosed daily.<br>**透明度**：每日披露持仓。<br>**Accessibility**: Available through brokerage accounts; fractional shares possible.<br>**可及性**：可通过经纪账户购买；可能支持碎股。<br>**Tax Benefits**: Fewer taxable events.<br>**税务优惠**：应税事件较少。 | **Risks and Disadvantages:**<br>**风险和劣势：**<br>While beneficial, ETFs carry risks.<br>虽然有利，但 ETF 也有风险。<br>**Market Risk**: Value fluctuates with underlying assets.<br>**市场风险**：价值随标的资产波动。<br>**Liquidity Risk**: Less-traded ETFs may have wider bid-ask spreads.<br>**流动性风险**：交易较少的 ETF 可能有更宽的买卖价差。<br>**Tracking Error**: May not perfectly match the index due to fees or rebalancing.<br>**跟踪误差**：由于费用或再平衡，可能无法完美匹配指数。<br>**Overtrading**: Intraday trading can lead to impulsive decisions.<br>**过度交易**：日内交易可能导致冲动决策。<br>**Leveraged/Inverse ETFs**: Higher risk, not suitable for long-term holding.<br>**杠杆/反向 ETF**：风险较高，不适合长期持有。<br>**Concentration Risk**: Thematic ETFs may be volatile.<br>**集中度风险**：主题 ETF 可能波动较大。 |

## 3.7 **如何交易ETF（How to Invest in ETFs）**

* **Open a Brokerage Account**: Use platforms like MooMoo, Vanguard, Fidelity, or Charles Schwab for trades.
* **开设经纪账户**：使用 MooMoo、Vanguard、Fidelity 或 Charles Schwab 等平台进行交易。

* **Search ETFs**: Use tools like MooMoo App, Morningstar or ETF.com to compare expense ratios, holdings, and performance.
* **搜索 ETF**：使用 MooMoo App、Morningstar 或 ETF.com 等工具比较费用率、持仓和表现。

* **Buy Shares**: Place orders via market, limit, or fractional shares.
* **购买份额**：通过市价单、限价单或碎股下单。

* **Diversify**: Start with broad-market ETFs (e.g., S\&P 500) for core holdings.
* **多元化**：从大盘 ETF（如 S\&P 500）开始作为核心持仓。

* **Monitor and Rebalance**: Review periodically but avoid frequent trading.
* **监控和再平衡**：定期审查但避免频繁交易。

* Regulation: ETFs are overseen by the SEC, ensuring transparency and investor protection.
* 监管：ETF 受 SEC 监管，确保透明度和投资者保护。

## 3.8 杠杆型和反向型ETF（ETFLeveraged and Inverse ETFs）

Leveraged and inverse ETFs are advanced products that use derivatives to amplify (e.g., 2x or 3x) or invert (e.g., -1x or -2x) the daily returns of an underlying index, sector, or asset. They are suited for short-term trading, not long-term holding, due to daily rebalancing and volatility decay. In 2025, the market grew significantly, with over 100 new launches, especially in AI and single-stock themes, reaching substantial AUM in targeted sectors.

杠杆和反向 ETF 是使用衍生品来放大（如 2 倍或 3 倍）或反向（如 -1 倍或 -2 倍）标的指数、行业或资产的每日回报的高级产品。由于每日再平衡和波动率损耗，它们适合短期交易，不适合长期持有。2025 年，该市场显著增长，推出了 100 多只新产品，特别是在 AI 和单只股票主题方面，在目标板块达到了可观的资产管理规模。

| **Key Types：**<br>**主要类型：**<br>**1.Leveraged (Bull)**: Aim for multiples of positive daily returns (e.g., ProShares Ultra S\&P 500 - SSO, 2x S\&P 500).<br>**1. 杠杆（看多）**：旨在获得每日正回报的倍数（例如 ProShares Ultra S\&P 500 - SSO，2倍 S\&P 500）。<br>**2.Inverse (Bear)**: Provide opposite returns (e.g., ProShares UltraShort Ether - ETHD, -2x Ethereum).<br>**2. 反向（看空）**：提供相反的回报（例如 ProShares UltraShort Ether - ETHD，-2倍以太坊）。<br>**3.Thematic/Single-Stock**: Focus on sectors like AI (e.g., GraniteShares 2x Long NVDA) or commodities (e.g., MicroSectors Gold Miners 3X - GDXU).<br>**3. 主题/单只股票**：专注于 AI 等板块（例如 GraniteShares 2x Long NVDA）或大宗商品（例如 MicroSectors Gold Miners 3X - GDXU）。 | **Advantages：**<br>**优势：**<br><ul><li>Amplified short-term gains and hedging without margin.</li><li>放大的短期收益和无需保证金的对冲。</li><li>High liquidity for tactical trades.</li><li>战术交易的高流动性。</li></ul> | **Risks：**<br>**风险：**<br><ul><li>Compounding effects erode long-term performance.</li><li>复利效应侵蚀长期表现。</li><li>Higher fees (0.9-1.5%) and counterparty risks.</li><li>较高的费用 (0.9-1.5%) 和交易对手风险。</li><li>Not for beginners; SEC warns of volatility.</li><li>不适合初学者；SEC 警告波动性。</li></ul> |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |

#### **Deep Analysis: Why Leveraged ETFs Are Not Suitable for Long-Term Holding:**
#### **深度分析：为什么杠杆 ETF 不适合长期持有：**

Leveraged ETFs (Exchange-Traded Funds) are financial instruments designed to deliver amplified daily returns based on an underlying index or asset. For example, a 2x leveraged ETF aims to provide twice the daily performance of its benchmark, while a 3x version targets three times. These products use derivatives like swaps, futures, and options to achieve this leverage, and they reset their exposure daily to maintain the target multiple. While they can be powerful tools for short-term trading or hedging, they are generally unsuitable for long-term holding due to a phenomenon known as **volatility decay** (also called volatility drag or beta slippage). This decay erodes the fund's value over time, especially in volatile or sideways markets, leading to returns that are significantly lower than what an investor might naively expect from simply multiplying the underlying asset's long-term return by the leverage factor.

杠杆 ETF（交易所交易基金）是一种金融工具，旨在根据标的指数或资产提供放大的每日回报。例如，2 倍杠杆 ETF 旨在提供其基准每日表现的两倍，而 3 倍版本的目标是三倍。这些产品使用掉期、期货和期权等衍生品来实现这种杠杆作用，并且它们每天重置敞口以维持目标倍数。虽然它们可以是短期交易或对冲的有力工具，但由于被称为**波动率损耗**（也称为波动率拖累或贝塔滑点）的现象，它们通常不适合长期持有。这种损耗会随着时间的推移侵蚀基金的价值，尤其是在波动或横盘整理的市场中，导致回报率大大低于投资者简单地将标的资产的长期回报率乘以杠杆倍数所预期的回报率。

In this analysis, I'll break down the concept of volatility decay step-by-step, explain its mathematical underpinnings, illustrate it with examples (including a simulated scenario), discuss its long-term implications, and touch on when leveraged ETFs might still be appropriate. The focus will be on how daily compounding and market fluctuations create this structural drag, making these ETFs a poor choice for buy-and-hold strategies.
在此分析中，我将逐步分解波动率损耗的概念，解释其数学基础，用示例（包括模拟场景）进行说明，讨论其长期影响，并谈谈何时杠杆 ETF 可能仍然合适。重点是每日复利和市场波动如何产生这种结构性拖累，使这些 ETF 成为买入并持有策略的糟糕选择。

1. **Understanding Leveraged ETFs and Their Daily Reset Mechanism**
1. **理解杠杆 ETF 及其每日重置机制**

To grasp volatility decay, it's essential to understand how leveraged ETFs operate. Unlike traditional ETFs, which track an index passively over time, leveraged ETFs are engineered for **daily** performance. Each day, the fund adjusts its holdings to ensure the next day's return is approximately the leverage multiple (e.g., 2x) of the underlying index's return, minus fees and expenses.
要掌握波动率损耗，必须了解杠杆 ETF 的运作方式。与随时间被动追踪指数的传统 ETF 不同，杠杆 ETF 是为**每日**表现而设计的。每一天，基金都会调整其持仓，以确保第二天的回报率大约是标的指数回报率的杠杆倍数（例如 2 倍），减去费用和开支。

* **Daily Reset Example:** If the underlying index rises 1% on Day 1, a 2x leveraged ETF should rise about 2%. If the index then falls 1% on Day 2, the ETF falls about 2%. This reset happens every trading day, leading to compounding effects over multiple periods.
* **每日重置示例**：如果标的指数在第一天上涨 1%，则 2 倍杠杆 ETF 应上涨约 2%。如果指数随后在第二天如跌 1%，则 ETF 下跌约 2%。这种重置发生在每个交易日，导致多个周期的复利效应。

* **Key Insight:** The ETF's performance is path-dependent. It doesn't aim to deliver 2x the return over a week, month, or year—only over each single day. Over longer horizons, the cumulative return deviates from the simple multiple due to the interaction between leverage and volatility.
* **关键见解**：ETF 的表现是路径依赖的。它的目标不是在一周、一个月或一年内提供 2 倍的回报——而仅在每一天。在较长的时间范围内，由于杠杆和波动率之间的相互作用，累计回报会偏离简单的倍数。

  This daily reset is the root cause of volatility decay. In a perfectly linear, upward-trending market with no volatility, a leveraged ETF would perform as expected (e.g., 2x the long-term return). However, real markets are volatile, and this volatility introduces a mathematical drag that compounds negatively over time.
  这种每日重置是波动率损耗的根本原因。在没有波动的完美线性上涨市场中，杠杆 ETF 的表现将符合预期（例如，长期回报的 2 倍）。然而，真实市场是波动的，这种波动引入了一种数学拖累，随着时间的推移产生负面复利。

2. **The Concept of Volatility Decay**
2. **波动率损耗的概念**

Volatility decay occurs because leveraged returns amplify both gains *and* losses, but losses have a disproportionately larger impact due to the nature of percentage-based compounding. In mathematical terms, when an asset experiences volatility (ups and downs), the geometric mean return (which determines compounded growth) is always less than or equal to the arithmetic mean return, with the difference increasing as volatility rises. Leverage magnifies this gap.
波动率损耗的发生是因为杠杆回报同时放大了收益*和*损失，但由于基于百分比的复利的性质，损失具有不成比例的更大影响。用数学术语来说，当资产经历波动（上涨和下跌）时，几何平均回报率（决定复利增长）总是小于或等于算术平均回报率，差异随着波动率的上升而增加。杠杆放大了这一差距。

* **Core Mechanism:** Consider a simple two-day cycle where the underlying index goes up by *x*% and then down by approximately *x*% to return to its starting point. The underlying ends flat, but the leveraged ETF ends lower because the amplified loss on the down day is calculated on a higher base (after the up day).
* **核心机制**：考虑一个简单的两天周期，其中标的指数上涨 *x*% 左右，然后下跌约 *x*% 回到起点。标的持平，但杠杆 ETF 收低，因为下跌日的放大损失是基于较高的基数（上涨日之后）计算的。

* **Why It Happens:** Gains and losses are not symmetric in percentage terms. To recover from a loss, you need a larger percentage gain. Leverage exacerbates this asymmetry.
* **发生原因**：收益和损失在百分比上是不对称的。要从损失中恢复，你需要更大的百分比收益。杠杆加剧了这种不对称性。

* **Table: The power of volatility decay, and the performance of leverage ETF under diffierent path:(source: leverage for the long run, Michael A. Gayed)**
* **表格：波动率损耗的力量，以及杠杆 ETF 在不同路径下的表现：（来源：leverage for the long run, Michael A. Gayed）**

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-34.png>)

#### **Leveraged ETFs with Built-In Strategies**
#### **具有内置策略的杠杆 ETF**

Leveraged ETFs are exchange-traded funds designed to amplify the daily returns of an underlying index, asset, or sector by a multiple (e.g., 2x or 3x) using derivatives like futures, swaps, or options. While traditional leveraged ETFs focus solely on this fixed daily leverage with a reset mechanism, those with **built-in strategies** incorporate additional layers of active or rules-based management to enhance performance, manage risk, or generate extra income. These strategies can include trend-following, volatility targeting, options overlays (e.g., covered calls), AI-driven adjustments, or dynamic allocation rules. The goal is often to mitigate issues like volatility decay (the erosion of returns over time due to compounding in volatile markets) or to adapt to changing market conditions, making them potentially more suitable for tactical or longer-term use compared to plain-vanilla leveraged products.
杠杆 ETF 是交易所交易基金，旨在通过使用期货、掉期或期权等衍生品将标的指数、资产或行业的每日回报放大数倍（例如 2 倍或 3 倍）。虽然传统杠杆 ETF 仅专注于这种具有重置机制的固定每日杠杆，但那些具有**内置策略**的 ETF 包含了额外的积极或基于规则的管理层，以增强表现、管理风险或产生额外收入。这些策略可以包括趋势跟踪、波动率目标、期权覆盖（例如备兑看涨）、AI 驱动的调整或动态分配规则。其目标通常是减轻波动率损耗（由于波动市场中的复利导致回报随时间侵蚀）等问题，或适应不断变化的市场条件，使其比普通杠杆产品更适合战术或长期使用。

These ETFs are typically aimed at experienced traders or investors seeking amplified exposure with some embedded intelligence, but they still carry high risks due to leverage, including magnified losses, higher fees (often 0.9-1.5%), and path dependency. They are not meant for passive, long-term buy-and-hold without monitoring, as the built-in strategies may not always outperform in all environments.
这些 ETF 通常针对经验丰富的交易员或寻求具有某种嵌入式智能的放大敞口的投资者，但由于杠杆作用，它们仍然具有高风险，包括放大的损失、较高的费用（通常为 0.9-1.5%）和路径依赖性。它们不适合在没有监控的情况下进行被动、长期买入并持有，因为内置策略可能并不总能在所有环境中跑赢大盘。

#### **Key Features of Built-In Strategies**
#### **内置策略的关键特征**

* **Trend-Following or Timing**: Some ETFs use signals like moving averages (e.g., 200-day SMA) to adjust leverage exposure, reducing it during downtrends to avoid prolonged decay. For example, strategies might rotate between leveraged positions and cash or unleveraged assets based on market momentum.
* **趋势跟踪或择时**：一些 ETF 使用移动平均线（例如 200 日均线）等信号来调整杠杆敞口，在下跌趋势中减少敞口以避免长期损耗。例如，策略可能会根据市场动量在杠杆头寸和现金或非杠杆资产之间轮换。

* **Options-Based Overlays**: These incorporate selling options (e.g., covered calls) on top of the leveraged base to generate yield or buffer downside. This can provide income boosts but may cap upside potential.
* **基于期权的覆盖**：这些策略在杠杆基础之上结合卖出期权（例如备兑看涨）以产生收益或缓冲下行风险。这可以提供收入提升，但可能会限制上行潜力。

* **Diversification or Hedging**: Built-in rules might blend leveraged equities with bonds, commodities, or inverse positions for risk parity, rebalancing periodically to harvest volatility premiums.
* **多元化或对冲**：内置规则可能会混合杠杆股票与债券、商品或反向头寸以实现风险平价，定期再平衡以获取波动率溢价。

  * **Examples of Leveraged ETFs with Built-In Strategies**
  * **具有内置策略的杠杆 ETF 示例**

  Here are a few notable types and products (as of early 2026; availability and performance can vary):
  以下是一些值得注意的类型和产品（截至 2026 年初；可用性和表现可能会有所不同）：

* **GraniteShares YieldBoost Series**: Combines 2x leverage on single stocks or indices with options-selling strategies to enhance yields. For instance, their offerings marry leveraged ETFs with covered calls for income generation while pursuing amplified returns.
* **GraniteShares YieldBoost 系列**：将单只股票或指数的 2 倍杠杆与期权卖出策略相结合以提高收益率。例如，他们的产品将杠杆 ETF 与备兑看涨相结合，在追求放大回报的同时产生收入。

* **Teucrium 2x Daily ETFs**: For commodities like wheat (WXET) or corn (CXRN), these provide 2x daily futures exposure with built-in rolling strategies to manage contango/decay in futures markets.
* **Teucrium 2 倍每日 ETF**：对于小麦 (WXET) 或玉米 (CXRN) 等商品，这些产品提供 2 倍每日期货敞口，并带有内置滚动策略以管理期货市场中的升水/损耗。

> **Case Study: AMYY ETF: designed to generate income through options strategies, primarily by selling put options on leveraged ETFs that track the 2x Long AMD Daily ETF.**
> **案例研究：AMYY ETF：旨在通过期权策略产生收入，主要是通过卖出追踪 2 倍做多 AMD 每日 ETF 的杠杆 ETF 的看跌期权。**

The FED (Federal Reserve) manages monetary policy, andNYSE is an exchange, not a regulator.<br />**解释：** 材料明确指出“ETF 受 SEC 监管，确保透明度和投资者保护”，因此 SEC 是监管机构。FED（美联储）管理货币政策，而 NYSE 是交易所，不是监管机构。 |

Long-term holding (years)&#xA;B. 长期持有（数年）<br />[C. No](http://C.No) time restriction<br />C. 无时间限制 | **Answer:A**&#xA;**答案：A**<br />**Explanation:** everaged ETFs are designed for short-term use due to daily rebalancing and volatility decay. Thematerial explicitly notes they are "unsuitable for long-term holding," eliminating B and C.<br />**解释：** 杠杆 ETF 由于每日再平衡和波动率损耗，专为短期使用而设计。材料明确指出它们“不适合长期持有”，排除了 B 和 C。 |

Highly volatile (ups and downs) markets&#xA;A. 高度波动（起伏）的市场<br />B. Steadily rising markets&#xA;B. 稳步上涨的市场<br />C.Steadily falling markets&#xA;C. 稳步下跌的市场 | **Answer: A**&#xA;**答案：A**<br />**Explanation:** Volatility decay stems from compounding effects of daily ups and downs-losses have adisproportionate impact on leveraged returns. The material emphasizes decay is worse in "volatile or sidewaysmarkets," while steady trends (A/B) minimize this drag.<br />**解释：** 波动率损耗源于每日起伏的复利效应——损失对杠杆回报有不成比例的影响。材料强调损耗在“波动或横盘整理的市场”中更为严重，而稳定趋势 (A/B) 可最大限度地减少这种拖累。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
<tr>
<td><strong>Straddle</strong><br>**跨式组合**</td>
<td>Buy call and put at same strike and expiration. Payoff: profits from large move either way, loss limited to net premium if no move. <br />买入相同行权价和到期日的看涨和看跌期权。收益：无论向哪个方向大幅波动均可获利，若无波动则损失限于净权利金。<br /><strong>Purpose:</strong> To profit from volatility regardless of direction. <br />**目的：** 无论方向如何，从波动中获利。<br /><strong>Market Environment:</strong> High volatility anticipated, direction uncertain, like earnings announcements. <br />**市场环境：** 预期高波动，方向不确定，如财报发布。<br /><strong>Real Market Example:</strong> Buy June 2026 $260 call for $18.2, $260 put for $16.5. Net cost $34.70. If AAPL goes to $300, profits from call is $40, strategy profit is $40 - $34.70 = $5.3. If Apple goes to $220, put profits is $ 40, strategy profit is 40-34.7-$5.3 as well. If $260, both worthless, loss $34.70.<br>**真实市场示例：** 以 18.2 美元买入 2026 年 6 月 260 美元看涨期权，以 16.5 美元买入 260 美元看跌期权。净成本 34.70 美元。如果 AAPL 涨至 300 美元，看涨期权获利 40 美元，策略利润为 40 - 34.70 = 5.3 美元。如果苹果跌至 220 美元，看跌期权获利 40 美元，策略利润也是 40 - 34.7 = 5.3 美元。如果为 260 美元，两者均作废，损失 34.70 美元。</td>

<td><strong> Strangle</strong><br>**宽跨式组合**</td>
<td>Buy call and put with same expiration but different strikes (call higher, put lower). Cheaper than straddle. Payoff: profits from even larger moves. <br />买入相同到期日但不同行权价（看涨较高，看跌较低）的看涨和看跌期权。比跨式组合便宜。收益：从更大的波动中获利。<br /><strong>Purpose:</strong> Similar to straddle but with lower cost, requiring bigger price swings. <br />**目的：** 类似于跨式组合，但成本较低，需要更大的价格波动。<br /><strong>Market Environment:</strong> Expected high volatility but with a wider neutral range. <br />**市场环境：** 预期高波动但中性范围更宽。<br /><strong>Real Market Example:</strong> Buy June 2026 $270 call for $13.38, $250 put for $12.25. Maximum loss limits to the strategy cost.<br>**真实市场示例：** 以 13.38 美元买入 2026 年 6 月 270 美元看涨期权，以 12.25 美元买入 250 美元看跌期权。净成本 25.63 美元。如果 AAPL 300 美元，看涨期权 30 美元，利润 30 - 25.63 = 4.37 美元。如果 220 美元，看跌期权 30 美元，相同。盈亏平衡点上方 295.63 美元，下方 224.37 美元。最大损失限于策略成本。</td>

### 4.4.3 末日期权（**Zero Days to Expiration (0DTE) Options（Source From Schwab and MooMoo, Not From CFA, Just need to know basic information)**）

Zero Days to Expiration (0DTE) options are options contracts that expire at the end of the same trading day they are purchased or sold. Introduced broadly in 2022 by exchanges like the Cboe, they have exploded in popularity, now accounting for about 50% of daily S\&P 500 index options volume. These are typically available on major indices and ETFs, such as SPX (S\&P 500), SPY (S\&P 500 ETF), NDX (Nasdaq-100), and QQQ (Nasdaq-100 ETF), with expirations offered on specific days like Mondays, Wednesdays, and Fridays for SPX, and daily for SPY and QQQ. Unlike longer-dated options, 0DTEs have no time value beyond the current session, making them hyper-sensitive to immediate price moves.

末日期权 (0DTE) 是指在买入或卖出当天收盘时到期的期权合约。自 2022 年由 Cboe 等交易所广泛推出以来，它们的人气爆发式增长，目前约占标准普尔 500 指数期权日交易量的 50%。这些期权通常适用于主要指数和 ETF，如 SPX（标普 500）、SPY（标普 500 ETF）、NDX（纳斯达克 100）和 QQQ（纳斯达克 100 ETF），SPX 的到期日为周一、周三和周五，SPY 和 QQQ 则为每日。与长期期权不同，0DTE 在当前交易时段之外没有时间价值，这使得它们对即时价格变动极度敏感。

* **Call Options in 0DTE**
* **0DTE 中的看涨期权**

A 0DTE call gives the buyer the right to buy the underlying at the strike price by day's end. Payoff: max (0, S\_T - X), where S\_T is the closing price.
0DTE 看涨期权赋予买方在当天结束前以行权价买入标的资产的权利。收益：max (0, S\_T - X)，其中 S\_T 是收盘价。

If ITM (S\_T > X): Exercise for intrinsic value minus premium; profits from quick upside spikes.
如果是实值 (ITM) (S\_T > X)：行权获得内在价值减去权利金；从快速上涨中获利。

If ATM/OTM (S\_T ≤ X): Expires worthless; full premium lost.
如果是平值/虚值 (ATM/OTM) (S\_T ≤ X)：过期作废；损失全部权利金。

For sellers: Collect premium but risk assignment if ITM, with unlimited upside loss potential in volatile markets.
对于卖方：收取权利金，但如果是 ITM 则面临被指派的风险，在波动市场中具有无限的上行损失潜力。

* **Real Market Example:** With SPY at $550 intraday, buy a 0DTE $552 call for $0.50 premium. If SPY closes at $555, payoff $3, net profit $2.50. If below $552, lose $0.50 fully.
* **真实市场示例：** SPY 日内价格为 550 美元，以 0.50 美元权利金买入 0DTE 552 美元看涨期权。如果 SPY 收于 555 美元，收益 3 美元，净利润 2.50 美元。如果低于 552 美元，损失全部 0.50 美元。

* **Put Options in 0DTE**
* **0DTE 中的看跌期权**

A 0DTE put allows selling the underlying at the strike by close. Payoff: max(0, X - S\_T).
0DTE 看跌期权允许在收盘前以行权价卖出标的资产。收益：max(0, X - S\_T)。

If ITM (S\_T < X): Exercise for value minus premium; gains from rapid drops.
如果是 ITM (S\_T < X)：行权获得价值减去权利金；从快速下跌中获利。

If ATM/OTM (S\_T ≥ X): Worthless; premium gone.
如果是 ATM/OTM (S\_T ≥ X)：作废；权利金损失。

Sellers: Keep premium if unexercised, but must buy at strike if assigned, risking down to zero.
卖方：如果未行权则保留权利金，但如果被指派则必须以行权价买入，风险直至归零。

* **Real Market Example:** SPY at $550, buy 0DTE $548 put for $0.40. If closes at $545, payoff $3, net $2.60. If above $548, lose $0.40.
* **真实市场示例：** SPY 为 550 美元，以 0.40 美元买入 0DTE 548 美元看跌期权。如果收于 545 美元，收益 3 美元，净利 2.60 美元。如果高于 548 美元，损失 0.40 美元。

* **Settlement and Key Points**
* **结算和关键点**

  * **Trading Hours:** Trade until 4:00 PM ET; no after-hours for 0DTEs. Settlement uses closing price.
  * **交易时间：** 交易至美国东部时间下午 4:00；0DTE 没有盘后交易。结算使用收盘价。

  * **Type:** Mostly cash-settled for indices (SPX/NDX), physical for ETFs (SPY/QQQ).
  * **类型：** 指数 (SPX/NDX) 大多为现金结算，ETF (SPY/QQQ) 为实物结算。

  * **Auto-Exercise:** ITM by $0.01+ auto-exercised; OTM expire automatically.
  * **自动行权：** ITM 超过 0.01 美元自动行权；OTM 自动过期。

  * **Greeks Impact:** Extreme theta decay (value erodes rapidly) and high gamma (sensitive to small moves), ideal for day trading but punishing for holders.
  * **希腊字母影响：** 极端的 Theta 衰减（价值迅速侵蚀）和高 Gamma（对小幅波动敏感），适合日内交易但对持有者不利。

* **Risks**
* **风险**

> **High Volatility:** Prices can swing wildly; 90%+ expire worthless, per studies.
> **高波动性：** 价格可能剧烈波动；研究显示 90% 以上过期作废。
>
> **Leverage Amplifies Losses:** Low premiums mean cheap entry, but total loss is common; no time for recovery.
> **杠杆放大损失：** 低权利金意味着低成本入场，但全额损失很常见；没有恢复时间。
>
> **Assignment/Pin Risk:** Near strikes at close can lead to unexpected assignment or gaps.
> **指派/钉住风险：** 收盘时接近行权价可能导致意外指派或缺口。

* **Market Impact:**&#x20;
* **市场影响：**

Heavy 0DTE volume can cause intraday volatility spikes or "gamma squeezes."
巨大的 0DTE 交易量可能导致日内波动率飙升或“Gamma 挤压”。

* **Market Environment：**
* **市场环境：**

0DTEs thrive in volatile sessions with news events (e.g., earnings, Fed announcements) for directional bets, or range-bound days for selling premium. Popular among retail for low-cost speculation but suit experienced day traders avoiding over-leverage.&#x20;
0DTE 在有新闻事件（如财报、美联储公告）的波动时段（用于方向性押注）或横盘整理日（用于卖出权利金）中表现活跃。受散户欢迎用于低成本投机，但适合避免过度杠杆的经验丰富的日内交易者。

| How to find and trade 0DTEs in MooMoo: 市场——期权——活跃末日期权: | ![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-80.png>) |
