| ** 7.What does the balance sheet represent?**<br />** 7. 资产负债表代表什么？**<br />A. Cash flows over a period<br />A. 一段时间内的现金流<br />B. A snapshot of assets, liabilities, and equity at a point in time<br />B. 特定时间点的资产、负债和权益的快照<br />C. Revenues and expenses over time<br />C. 随时间推移的收入和费用 | **Correct answer: B**<br />**正确答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** It follows the equation Assets = Liabilities + Equity, assessing solvency, liquidity, and capital structure.<br />**解释：** 它遵循等式资产 = 负债 + 权益，评估偿债能力、流动性和资本结构。 |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **8.What are assets on the balance sheet?**<br />**8. 资产负债表上的资产是什么？**<br />A. Obligations to pay<br />A. 支付义务<br />B. Resources expected to provide future economic benefits<br />B. 预期提供未来经济利益的资源<br />C. Shareholder dividends<br />C. 股东股息 | **Correct answer: B**<br />**正确答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** Assets include tangible and intangible items, measured at historical cost or fair value, representing value-generating capacity.<br />**解释：** 资产包括有形和无形项目，按历史成本或公允价值计量，代表价值创造能力。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **9.What is the difference between current and non-current liabilities?**<br />**9. 流动负债和非流动负债有什么区别？**<br />A. Current is long-term; non-current is short-term<br />A. 流动是长期的；非流动是短期的<br />B. Current are settled within one year; non-current beyond one year<br />B. 流动负债在一年内结算；非流动负债超过一年<br />C. Both are the same<br />C. 两者相同 | **Correct answer: B**<br />**正确答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** Current liabilities (e.g., accounts payable) indicate short-term health, while non-current (e.g., long-term debt) show longer-term obligations.<br />**解释：** 流动负债（如应付账款）表明短期健康状况，而非流动负债（如长期债务）显示长期义务。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |

| **10.What does Retained Earnings represent in equity?**<br />**10. 权益中的留存收益代表什么？**<br />A. Cumulative profits minus dividends<br />A. 累计利润减去股息<br />B. Par value of shares<br />B. 股票面值<br />C. Unrealized gains from investments<br />C. 投资的未实现收益 | **Correct answer: A**<br />**正确答案：A**<br />**Explanation:** It shows earnings reinvested in the business; a negative balance is Accumulated Deficit, indicating historical losses.<br />**解释：** 它显示再投资于企业的收益；负余额是累计亏损，表明历史亏损。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |

| **11.What is Treasury Stock?**<br />**11. 什么是库存股？**<br />A. Newly issued shares<br />A. 新发行的股票<br />B. Repurchased shares held by the company, deducted from equity<br />B. 公司持有的回购股票，从权益中扣除<br />C. Dividends paid out<br />C. 支付的股息 | **Correct answer: B**<br />**正确答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** It's a contra-equity account, reducing shareholders' equity as it represents non-outstanding shares.<br />**解释：** 它是一个权益抵减账户，减少股东权益，因为它代表非流通股。 |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |

| **12.What is Cash Flows from Operating Activities?**<br />**12. 经营活动产生的现金流量是什么？**<br />A. Cash from buying assets<br />A. 购买资产的现金<br />B. Net cash from core business operations, like sales<br />B. 核心业务运营（如销售）产生的净现金<br />C. Cash from issuing stock<br />C. 发行股票的现金 | **Correct answer: B**<br />**正确答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** It represents the cash version of operating profit, excluding non-cash items, indicating operational cash generation.<br />**解释：** 它代表经营利润的现金版本，不包括非现金项目，表明运营现金的产生。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **13.What is Return on Equity (ROE)?**<br />**13. 什么是净资产收益率 (ROE)？**<br />A. Net Income / Revenue<br />A. 净利润 / 收入<br />B. Net Income / Average Shareholders' Equity<br />B. 净利润 / 平均股东权益<br />C. Revenue - Expenses<br />C. 收入 - 费用 | **Correct answer: B**<br />**正确答案：B**<br />**Explanation:** ROE measures profit generation from equity, indicating management efficiency; high ROE suggests strong investment returns.<br />**解释：** ROE 衡量权益产生的利润，表明管理效率；高 ROE 表明投资回报强劲。 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
# 8. 研究报告:公司营收分析:行业分析 **Revenue Drivers, Company Research Reports and industry analysis**

> 考试小贴士：本章节题目占比为6-8%左右。
>
> * 8.1部分为重点考察对象，你需要了解卖方研究报告的大体结构、不同评级间的区别以及在MooMoo App中如何找到卖方评价相关信息。
>
> * 8.2-8.3为重点考察内容，你需要知道如何预测公司营收（Top-down和bottom-up两种方法的底层逻辑）以及如何运用波特五力模型分析行业竞争格局。
>
> * 8.4主要考察概念，对文件中的概念有初步印象即可。
>
> * 产品层面：如何在MooMoo App中找到公司的评级信息以及最新的评级变动、如何在App中找到公司所属的具体行业和板块。
>
>

## 8.1 **卖方公司研究报告 Sell-Side Company Research Reports**

Sell-side analysts (from investment banks or brokerages) produce these reports to guide client investment decisions. They combine fundamental analysis with recommendations (e.g., Buy, Hold, Sell) and target prices.
卖方分析师（来自投资银行或经纪公司）撰写这些报告，以指导客户的投资决策。他们结合基本面分析与建议（如买入、持有、卖出）和目标价格。

* **Purpose**: Provide independent insights on company fundamentals, industry dynamics, and valuation. Support client portfolio decisions and generate trading activity.
* **目的**：提供关于公司基本面、行业动态和估值的独立见解。支持客户投资组合决策并产生交易活动。

* **Typical Structure and Key Content**:
* **典型结构和关键内容**：

1. **Executive Summary / Investment Thesis** — Concise overview of recommendation, target price, and rationale.
1. **执行摘要 / 投资论点** — 简明扼要的建议、目标价格和理由概览。

2. **Company Overview** — Business model, products/services, and strategy.
2. **公司概览** — 商业模式、产品/服务和战略。

3. **Industry and Competitive Analysis** — Market trends, peers, and forces.
3. **行业和竞争分析** — 市场趋势、同行和竞争力量。

4. **Financial Review** — Historical results, revenue/profit drivers, margins.
4. **财务回顾** — 历史业绩、收入/利润驱动因素、利润率。

5. **Forecasts and Valuation** — Revenue/earnings projections, DCF or multiples-based valuation.
5. **预测和估值** — 收入/收益预测、基于 DCF 或倍数的估值。

6. **Risks and Catalysts** — Key risks and upside/downside scenarios.
6. **风险和催化剂** — 关键风险和上行/下行场景。

7. **Recommendation and Target Price** — With upside/downside potential.
7. **建议和目标价格** — 具有上行/下行潜力。

Example：GS's latest research report on Apple
示例：高盛关于苹果的最新研究报告

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-98.png>)

Analyst Ratings (Buy, Hold, Sell): Analyst recommendations are a key feature of sell-side reports, typically expressed as one of three core ratings: Buy, Hold, or Sell. These are based on the analyst's assessment of the stock's expected performance relative to a benchmark (e.g., market index like S\&P 500) over a specified horizon (usually 12 months). Ratings are tied to the target price, which is derived from valuation models, and must include the firm's rating system definition for transparency.&#x20;
分析师评级（买入、持有、卖出）：分析师建议是卖方报告的关键特征，通常表示为三个核心评级之一：买入、持有或卖出。这些是基于分析师对股票在特定时间范围（通常为 12 个月）内相对于基准（例如标准普尔 500 指数等市场指数）的预期表现的评估。评级与源自估值模型的目标价格挂钩，并且必须包括公司的评级系统定义以确保透明度。

Buy (or Overweight/Outperform/Strong Buy): This rating indicates the analyst expects the stock to significantly outperform the market or its benchmark. Typically, this means anticipated total return (price appreciation + dividends) of 10–20% or more above the benchmark's expected return. For example, if the benchmark is projected to return 8%, a Buy might imply >18% total return for the stock. It signals strong conviction in upside drivers like revenue growth, margin expansion, or undervaluation. Investors are encouraged to purchase or add to positions. In reports, Buys are supported by bullish theses, such as a sustainable competitive moat or favorable industry trends.
买入（或增持/跑赢大盘/强力买入）：此评级表明分析师预计该股票将显着跑赢市场或其基准。通常，这意味着预期的总回报（价格增值 + 股息）比基准的预期回报高出 10-20% 或更多。例如，如果基准预计回报率为 8%，则“买入”可能意味着该股票的总回报率 >18%。这标志着对收入增长、利润率扩张或低估等上行驱动因素的坚定信念。鼓励投资者购买或增加头寸。在报告中，“买入”得到看涨论点的支持，例如可持续的竞争护城河或有利的行业趋势。

Hold (or Neutral/Market Perform): A neutral stance where the analyst expects the stock to perform in line with the market or benchmark, with limited upside or downside (e.g., total return within ±5–10% of the benchmark). This rating suggests the stock is fairly valued at current levels, with balanced risks and rewards. It might be used when the company is stable but lacks catalysts for outperformance, or when awaiting more data (e.g., upcoming earnings). Investors are advised to maintain existing positions but not aggressively buy or sell. Holds are the most common rating, often reflecting caution or equilibrium.
持有（或中性/市场表现）：一种中立立场，分析师预计该股票的表现将与市场或基准一致，上行或下行空间有限（例如，总回报在基准的 ±5-10% 以内）。此评级表明该股票在当前水平上估值合理，风险和回报平衡。当公司稳定但缺乏跑赢大盘的催化剂，或等待更多数据（如即将发布的财报）时，可能会使用此评级。建议投资者维持现有头寸，不要激进买卖。“持有”是最常见的评级，通常反映谨慎或平衡。

Sell (or Underweight/Underperform/Strong Sell): This signals the analyst expects the stock to underperform the market or benchmark, often with significant downside (e.g., total return 10–20% or more below the benchmark). Reasons could include overvaluation, deteriorating fundamentals (e.g., weakening revenue drivers, competitive threats), or high risks. Investors are recommended to reduce or exit positions. Sells are rarer (often <10% of ratings due to potential backlash from covered companies), but they demonstrate independent analysis. Reports with Sells emphasize quantified risks and alternative investments.
卖出（或减持/跑输大盘/强力卖出）：这表明分析师预计该股票将跑输市场或基准，通常具有显著的下行空间（例如，总回报比基准低 10-20% 或更多）。原因可能包括估值过高、基本面恶化（例如，收入驱动因素减弱、竞争威胁）或高风险。建议投资者减少或退出头寸。“卖出”较少见（由于可能受到被覆盖公司的反弹，通常占评级的 <10%），但它们证明了独立分析。带有“卖出”评级的报告强调量化风险和替代投资。

## 8.2 **收入分析与收入驱动因素 Revenue Analysis and Revenue Drivers**

Revenue drivers are factors that causally influence revenue levels and growth. Analysts start by analyzing historical revenues to identify these drivers, then assess their stability and predictability for forecasting purposes.
收入驱动因素是因果影响收入水平和增长的因素。分析师首先分析历史收入以确定这些驱动因素，然后评估其稳定性和可预测性以用于预测目的。

Bottom-Up Approach (Decomposing Revenues Internally): This method breaks down revenues into granular components such as sales volume and price, or by product line, segment, geography, or other logical categories. It is often the preferred starting point because it aligns directly with the company's operations and business model. Further decomposition can reveal sub-drivers (e.g., number of stores × sales per store).
自下而上法（内部拆解收入）：这种方法将收入分解为细粒度的组成部分，如销量和价格，或按产品线、分部、地理区域或其他逻辑类别分解。这通常是首选的起点，因为它直接与公司的运营和商业模式保持一致。进一步分解可以揭示子驱动因素（例如，门店数量 × 单店销售额）。

**Key Steps:**
**关键步骤：**

1. Identify primary revenue streams from financial statements (e.g., net sales, membership fees).
1. 从财务报表中识别主要收入来源（例如，净销售额、会员费）。

2. Decompose each stream into drivers (e.g., average units × average price).
2. 将每个来源分解为驱动因素（例如，平均单位数 × 平均价格）。

3. Calculate historical changes in drivers (absolute and percentage) to assess trends and stability.
3. 计算驱动因素的历史变化（绝对值和百分比）以评估趋势和稳定性。

4. Watch for signs of predictability (stable drivers) or volatility (external influences).
4. 观察可预测性（稳定驱动因素）或波动性（外部影响）的迹象。

Case Study: Starbucks Corporation (Bottom-Up Example): Starbucks operates a global coffeehouse chain with revenues primarily from company-operated stores (beverages, food, and other items). Key drivers include the number of stores, comparable store sales growth (same-store sales, or SSS, which measures performance of stores open at least 13 months), and average ticket size (spend per transaction).
案例研究：星巴克公司（自下而上示例）：星巴克经营全球咖啡连锁店，收入主要来自直营店（饮料、食品和其他商品）。关键驱动因素包括门店数量、可比门店销售增长（同店销售额，或 SSS，衡量开业至少 13 个月的门店业绩）和平均客单价（每笔交易支出）。

From 2013 to 2023, revenue from company-operated stores grew from $11.98B to $29.46B (146%), driven by a 92% increase in stores and cumulative SSS growth.
从 2013 年到 2023 年，直营店收入从 119.8 亿美元增长到 294.6 亿美元 (146%)，这得益于门店数量增加 92% 和累计 SSS 增长。

| **Year (Fiscal)**<br>**年份 (财政)** | **Company-Operated Stores (End of Year)**<br>**直营店 (年末)** | **Net New Stores**<br>**净新开门店** | **Average Stores**<br>**平均门店数** | **Comparable Store Sales Growth (%)**<br>**可比门店销售增长 (%)** | **Revenue from Company-Operated Stores (USD B)**<br>**直营店收入 (十亿美元)** | **Revenue Growth (%)**<br>**收入增长 (%)** |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 2013 | 10,194 | 1,701 | \~9,694 | 7% | 11.98 | 12% |
| 2014 | 10,713 | 519 | \~10,454 | 6% | 12.98 | 8% |
| 2015 | 12,235 | 1,522 | \~11,474 | 7% | 16.45 | 27% |
| 2016 | 12,711 | 476 | \~12,473 | 3% | 17.30 | 5% |
| 2017 | 13,930 | 1,219 | \~13,321 | 3% | 18.41 | 6% |
| 2018 | 15,341 | 1,411 | \~14,636 | 2% | 20.48 | 11% |
| 2019 | 15,921 | 580 | \~15,631 | 5% | 21.96 | 7% |
| 2020 | 15,863 | -58 | \~15,892 | -14% | 19.16 | -13% |
| 2021 | 15,444 | -419 | \~15,654 | 20% | 24.57 | 28% |
| 2022 | 18,253 | 2,809 | \~16,849 | 7% | 26.58 | 8% |
| 2023 | 19,592 | 1,339 | \~18,923 | 8% | 29.46 | 11% |

Top-Down Approach (Market Context): This complements bottom-up by expressing revenues as a function of external drivers like market size (addressable market or TAM), market share, and macroeconomic factors (e.g., GDP growth). It helps assess competitive positioning and growth potential.
自上而下法（市场背景）：通过将收入表示为市场规模（潜在市场或 TAM）、市场份额和宏观经济因素（如 GDP 增长）等外部驱动因素的函数，补充了自下而上法。它有助于评估竞争地位和增长潜力。

**Key Steps:**
**关键步骤：**

1. Define market size (include similar products; consider substitutes separately).
1. 定义市场规模（包括类似产品；单独考虑替代品）。

2. Calculate company share over time to identify gains/losses.
2. 计算公司随时间的份额以识别收益/损失。

3. Relate to macro drivers (e.g., retail sales growth ≈ GDP + inflation).
3. 与宏观驱动因素联系起来（例如，零售额增长 ≈ GDP + 通货膨胀）。

Case Study: Starbucks Corporation (Top-Down Example): The U.S. coffee shop industry (including chains and independents) provides context for Starbucks' North America segment, which accounts for 75% of global revenue.
案例研究：星巴克公司（自上而下示例）：美国咖啡店行业（包括连锁店和独立店）为星巴克的北美分部提供了背景，该分部占全球收入的 75%。

In top-down approach, you forecast the market size first(by macro drivers or industry trend), then make assumptions about the market shares to project the revenue.
在自上而下的方法中，你首先预测市场规模（通过宏观驱动因素或行业趋势），然后假设市场份额以预测收入。

## 8.3 **行业与竞争分析 Industry and Competitive Analysis**

**Porter's Five Forces Framework**:
**波特五力模型框架：**

1. Threat of new entrants.
1. 新进入者的威胁。

2. Bargaining power of suppliers.
2. 供应商的议价能力。

3. Bargaining power of buyers.
3. 买家的议价能力。

4. Threat of substitutes.
4. 替代品的威胁。

5. Rivalry among existing competitors.
5. 现有竞争者之间的竞争。

| Force (What to Ask)<br>力量（问什么） | Explanation<br>解释 | Case (Retail Industry: Walmart vs. Amazon)<br>案例（零售业：沃尔玛 vs 亚马逊） |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Threat of New Entrants (Can New Companies Easily Join the Industry?)<br>新进入者的威胁（新公司能轻易加入该行业吗？） | If it’s easy to start a company in the industry, competition increases (profits drop). If it’s hard (high costs, big barriers), competition is low (profits stay high).<br>如果在这个行业创业很容易，竞争就会加剧（利润下降）。如果很难（高成本、大壁垒），竞争就低（利润保持高位）。 | High barrier (hard to enter). To compete with Walmart/Amazon, a new retailer needs billions of dollars to build stores/warehouses, negotiate with suppliers, and build a customer base. So, threat of new entrants is LOW (good for Walmart/Amazon).<br>高壁垒（难以进入）。要与沃尔玛/亚马逊竞争，新的零售商需要数十亿美元来建立商店/仓库、与供应商谈判并建立客户群。因此，新进入者的威胁很低（对沃尔玛/亚马逊有利）。 |
| Supplier Power (Can Suppliers Raise Prices on Companies?)<br>供应商力量（供应商能对公司提价吗？） | If suppliers have few competitors (e.g., a unique product), they can raise prices (hurting company profits). If suppliers have many competitors, companies can force them to lower prices (helping profits).<br>如果供应商几乎没有竞争对手（例如独特的产品），他们可以提高价格（损害公司利润）。如果供应商有很多竞争对手，公司可以迫使他们降低价格（有助于利润）。 | Low supplier power. Walmart buys billions of dollars of products from suppliers like Procter & Gamble (P\&G). If P\&G raises prices, Walmart can switch to a cheaper supplier (or stop selling P\&G products). So P\&G has to offer Walmart favorable prices (good for Walmart’s profits).<br>供应商力量低。沃尔玛从宝洁 (P\&G) 等供应商处购买数十亿美元的产品。如果宝洁提价，沃尔玛可以转向更便宜的供应商（或停止销售宝洁产品）。所以宝洁必须向沃尔玛提供优惠价格（对沃尔玛的利润有利）。 |
| Buyer Power (Can Customers Force Companies to Lower Prices?)<br>买家力量（客户能迫使公司降价吗？） | If customers have many choices (e.g., many similar products), they can demand lower prices (profits drop). If customers have few choices, companies can charge more (profits rise).<br>如果客户有很多选择（例如许多类似产品），他们可以要求更低的价格（利润下降）。如果客户选择很少，公司可以收取更多费用（利润上升）。 | High buyer power. Consumers can easily switch between Walmart and Amazon—if Walmart’s price for a cereal box is $5 and Amazon’s is $4.50, most customers will buy from Amazon. So Walmart/Amazon have to keep prices low (hurting profits).<br>买家力量大。消费者可以轻松在沃尔玛和亚马逊之间切换——如果沃尔玛的一盒麦片价格是 5 美元，而亚马逊是 4.50 美元，大多数顾客会从亚马逊购买。所以沃尔玛/亚马逊必须保持低价（损害利润）。 |
| Threat of Substitutes (Can Customers Use Something Else Instead?)<br>替代品的威胁（客户可以使用其他东西代替吗？） | Substitutes = products/services that do the same thing. If there are many substitutes, companies lose customers (profits drop). If there are few substitutes, companies keep customers (profits rise).<br>替代品 = 做同样事情的产品/服务。如果有很多替代品，公司就会失去客户（利润下降）。如果替代品很少，公司就能留住客户（利润上升）。 | Moderate threat. Online shopping (Amazon) is a substitute for in-store shopping (Walmart). But Walmart’s grocery segment has low substitution—most people still buy groceries in-store (they want to pick fresh produce). So overall threat is moderate (not too bad for Walmart/Amazon).<br>威胁中等。在线购物（亚马逊）是店内购物（沃尔玛）的替代品。但沃尔玛的杂货业务替代性低——大多数人仍然在店内购买杂货（他们想挑选新鲜农产品）。所以总体威胁是中等的（对沃尔玛/亚马逊来说不算太坏）。 |
| Rivalry Among Existing Firms (How Hard Do Companies Compete with Each Other?)<br>现有公司之间的竞争（公司之间的竞争有多激烈？） | If there are many big companies fighting for market share (e.g., price wars, ads), profits drop. If there are few big companies, competition is low (profits rise).<br>如果有很多大公司争夺市场份额（例如价格战、广告），利润就会下降。如果大公司很少，竞争就低（利润上升）。 | High rivalry. Walmart and Amazon fight fiercely: Walmart uses "Everyday Low Prices" to attract in-store customers; Amazon uses Prime Day (big discounts) to attract online customers. They also copy each other (Walmart started online shopping, Amazon started physical stores). This high rivalry keeps profits in check (but both are big enough to still make money).<br>竞争激烈。沃尔玛和亚马逊竞争激烈：沃尔玛使用“天天低价”吸引店内顾客；亚马逊使用 Prime Day（大折扣）吸引在线顾客。他们还互相模仿（沃尔玛开始了在线购物，亚马逊开设了实体店）。这种激烈的竞争限制了利润（但两者都足够大，仍然可以赚钱）。 |

**Strong forces reduce industry profitability; weak forces enable higher margins.**
**强大的力量会降低行业盈利能力；弱小的力量有助于实现更高的利润率。**

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image8.jpeg>)

## 8.4 行业分类概述 Industry Classification Overview

Industry classification is the process of grouping companies that offer similar products or services. This helps analysts compare peers, forecast performance, and assess risks like competition or market trends. It's typically based on a "demand-side" view (what customers buy) rather than production methods.
行业分类是将提供类似产品或服务的公司进行分组的过程。这有助于分析师比较同行、预测业绩并评估竞争或市场趋势等风险。它通常基于“需求侧”观点（客户购买什么）而非生产方法。

### 8.4.1 美国上市公司所采用的分类标准 Classification Standards Used by US Stock Companies

For US-listed stocks (e.g., on NYSE or Nasdaq), the most common standard is the Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS), developed by MSCI and S\&P Dow Jones Indices. It's widely used for indexing (like S\&P 500 sectors), benchmarking, and research.
对于美国上市股票（例如在纽交所或纳斯达克上市），最常见的标准是由 MSCI 和标准普尔道琼斯指数开发的全球行业分类标准 (GICS)。它广泛用于指数编制（如标准普尔 500 行业）、基准测试和研究。

Hierarchy: GICS has 4 levels:
层级：GICS 有 4 个级别：

11 Sectors: Broad categories like Information Technology, Health Care, Financials, Consumer Discretionary, etc.
11 个板块：广泛的类别，如信息技术、医疗保健、金融、非必需消费品等。

25 Industry Groups: Subdivisions, e.g., Software & Services under Information Technology.
25 个行业组：细分，例如信息技术下的软件与服务。

74 Industries: More specific, e.g., Application Software.
74 个行业：更具体，例如应用软件。

163 Sub-Industries: Finest detail, e.g., Systems Software.
163 个子行业：最详细，例如系统软件。

![](<images/入门考试学习资料 Learning Resources for Entry Knowledge Test-image-112.png>)

Assignment Rules: Based on a company's primary revenue source (usually >50% from one business line). For multi-product firms like Amazon, it's classified under Consumer Discretionary (Broadline Retail) due to e-commerce dominance.
分配规则：基于公司的主要收入来源（通常 >50% 来自一条业务线）。对于亚马逊等多产品公司，由于电子商务的主导地位，它被归类为非必需消费品（综合零售）。

Other standards include:
其他标准包括：

SIC (Standard Industrial Classification): Older US government system (used in SEC filings), but less common now for analysis.
SIC（标准行业分类）：较旧的美国政府系统（用于 SEC 备案），但现在用于分析较少见。

NAICS (North American Industry Classification System): Replaces SIC for economic stats, focused on North America.
NAICS（北美行业分类系统）：取代 SIC 用于经济统计，专注于北美。

| How to find industry classification information in MooMoo: Select a company——公司——板块<br />如何在 MooMoo 中查找行业分类信息：选择公司——公司——板块 |   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | - |

# 9. 金融道德 **Financial Ethics**

> 考试小贴士：金融道德为必考题，以概念为主，占3%-5%左右。
>
> In this chapter, we mainly introduce the U.S. securities regulatory bodies, common financial misconduct, and the importance of understanding the regulatory framework to ensure compliance in daily work.
>
> 在本章中，主要介绍美国的证券监管机构以及常见的金融违规行为，保障大家在日常工作中了解监管机构和规范个人行为。

## 9.1 证券监管机构 Securities Regulatory Organizations

The U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) is the major watchdog of the securities industry. Congress created the SEC to regulate securities markets and to protect investors from fraudulent and manipulative practices. All broker–dealers who transact business with investors and other broker–dealers must register with the SEC. And that registration means something: All broker–dealers have to comply with SEC rules or face censure (an official reprimand), limits on activity, their own suspension or suspension of one or more associated persons (such as a registered rep or principal), a fine, and/or having their registration revoked.
美国证券交易委员会 (SEC) 是证券行业的主要监督者。国会创建了 SEC 来监管证券市场并保护投资者免受欺诈和操纵行为的侵害。所有与投资者和其他经纪交易商进行业务往来的经纪交易商都必须向 SEC 注册。这种注册意味着：所有经纪交易商都必须遵守 SEC 规则，否则将面临谴责（正式训斥）、活动限制、自身停业或一名或多名关联人员（如注册代表或负责人）停职、罚款和/或被撤销注册。

## 9.2 自律组织 Self-regulatory organizations

Due to the unscrupulous nature of some investors and registered representatives, the SEC’s job is overwhelming. Fortunately, a few self-regulatory organizations (SROs) are there to take some of the burden off of the SEC’s shoulders. Although membership isn’t mandatory, most broker–dealers are members of one or more SROs. SRO rules are usually stricter than those of the SEC. The four types of SROs we need to know are FINRA, MSRB, NYSE, and CBOE:
由于一些投资者和注册代表的不道德性质，SEC 的工作不堪重负。幸运的是，一些自律组织 (SRO) 可以分担 SEC 的部分负担。虽然会员资格不是强制性的，但大多数经纪交易商都是一个或多个 SRO 的成员。SRO 的规则通常比 SEC 的规则更严格。我们需要了解的四种 SRO 是 FINRA、MSRB、NYSE 和 CBOE：

**»»Financial Industry Regulatory Authority (FINRA):** FINRA is the SRO responsible for the operation and regulation of the OTC market, investment banking, NYSE trades, investment companies, limited partnerships, and so on. FINRA was created in 2007 and is a consolidation of the National Association of Securities Dealers (NASD) and the regulation and enforcement portions of the NYSE. FINRA is responsible for making sure that its members follow not only FINRA rules, but also the rules set forth by the SEC. Additionally, the FINRA is responsible for handling complaints against member firms and may take disciplinary action if necessary. FINRA is also responsible for administering securities exams such as the SIE. FINRA has strict rules regarding filing of misleading, incomplete, or inaccurate information concerning membership, the firm’s registration, and the registration of member associates.
**»»金融业监管局 (FINRA)：** FINRA 是负责场外交易 (OTC) 市场、投资银行、纽交所交易、投资公司、有限合伙企业等运作和监管的 SRO。FINRA 成立于 2007 年，是全国证券交易商协会 (NASD) 与纽交所监管和执法部门的合并。FINRA 负责确保其成员不仅遵守 FINRA 规则，还遵守 SEC 制定的规则。此外，FINRA 负责处理针对成员公司的投诉，并在必要时采取纪律处分。FINRA 还负责管理证券考试，如 SIE。FINRA 对于提交有关会员资格、公司注册和成员关联人员注册的误导性、不完整或不准确信息有严格的规定。

**»»Municipal Securities Rulemaking Board (MSRB):** The MSRB was established to develop rules that banks and securities firms have to follow when underwriting, selling, buying, and recommending municipal securities. The MSRB is subject to SEC oversight but does not enforce SEC rules. The MSRB makes rules for firms (and representatives) who sell municipal bonds but don’t enforce them; it leaves enforcement up to FINRA.
**»»市政证券规则制定委员会 (MSRB)：** MSRB 的成立是为了制定银行和证券公司在承销、销售、购买和推荐市政证券时必须遵守的规则。MSRB 受 SEC 监督，但不执行 SEC 规则。MSRB 为销售市政债券的公司（和代表）制定规则，但不执行这些规则；它将执法权留给 FINRA。

**»»New York Stock Exchange（NYSE）:** The NYSE is the oldest and largest stock exchange in the United States. The NYSE is responsible for listing securities, setting exchange policies, and supervising the exchange and member firms. The NYSE has the power to take disciplinary action against member firms.
**»»纽约证券交易所 (NYSE)：** 纽交所是美国最古老、最大的证券交易所。纽交所负责证券上市、制定交易所政策以及监督交易所和成员公司。纽交所有权对成员公司采取纪律处分。

**»»Chicago Board Options Exchange (CBOE):** The CBOE is an exchange that makes and enforces options exchange rules.
**»»芝加哥期权交易所 (CBOE)：** CBOE 是一个制定并执行期权交易规则的交易所。

## 9.3 常见的违规行为以及监管处罚 Common Violations and Regulatory Sanctions

In the United States, regulations targeting individual securities practitioners (such as brokers, investment advisors, analysts, etc.) are extremely stringent. The primary regulatory bodies include the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), the Financial Industry Regulatory Authority (FINRA), and the Department of Justice (DOJ).
在美国，针对个人证券从业人员（如经纪人、投资顾问、分析师等）的监管极为严格。主要监管机构包括美国证券交易委员会 (SEC)、金融业监管局 (FINRA) 和司法部 (DOJ)。

In the 2026 regulatory environment, the main violations for individual practitioners and their corresponding penalties are organized below:
在 2026 年的监管环境下，个人从业人员的主要违规行为及其相应处罚整理如下：

### 9.3.1 **内幕交易与非法信息泄露 Insider Trading & Illegal Information Disclosure (Tipping)**

* **Regulation**: Practitioners are strictly prohibited from using "material non-public information" (MNPI) obtained through their positions for trading, or from disclosing such information to others (tipping).
* **监管**：严禁从业人员利用职务之便获取的“重大非公开信息”(MNPI) 进行交易，或将此类信息透露给他人（泄露）。

* **Individual Penalties**:
* **个人处罚**：

  * **Civil Penalty**: The SEC can demand the disgorgement of all illegal profits and impose a fine of up to **3 times** the amount gained or loss avoided.
  * **民事处罚**：SEC 可以要求没收所有非法所得，并处以最高达获利或避免损失金额 **3 倍**的罚款。

  * **Criminal Penalty**: The DOJ can file criminal charges, leading to a maximum of **20 years** in federal prison and individual criminal fines of up to **$5 million**.
  * **刑事处罚**：司法部可以提起刑事指控，导致最高 **20 年**的联邦监禁和最高 **500 万美元**的个人刑事罚款。

  * **Industry Bar**: Permanent expulsion from the securities industry.
  * **行业禁入**：永久逐出证券行业。

### 9.3.2 **私下交易与外接业务 Selling Away & Outside Business Activities**

* **Regulation**: Practitioners may not privately sell securities products unauthorized by their firm ("Selling Away") or engage in undisclosed Outside Business Activities (OBA) without written approval.
* **监管**：未经书面批准，从业人员不得私自销售未经公司授权的证券产品（“私下交易”）或从事未披露的外部商业活动 (OBA)。

* **Individual Penalties**:
* **个人处罚**：

  * **FINRA Penalties**: Usually face suspensions ranging from several months to several years. If involved in a "Ponzi scheme" or serious fraud, they will be **permanently barred** from practice.
  * **FINRA 处罚**：通常面临数月至数年的停职。如果涉及“庞氏骗局”或严重欺诈，将被**永久禁止**从业。

  * **Firm Penalties**: The employing firm will usually terminate the individual immediately and record the violation on the U5 form (termination notice), making future employment difficult.
  * **公司处罚**：雇佣公司通常会立即解雇该个人，并将违规行为记录在 U5 表格（终止通知）上，导致未来就业困难。

### 9.3.3 **虚假陈述与误导性宣传 Misrepresentation & Omitting Facts**

* **Regulation**: Prohibited from concealing risks, exaggerating returns, or providing false financial data when selling securities to investors.
* **监管**：禁止在向投资者销售证券时隐瞒风险、夸大回报或提供虚假财务数据。

* **Individual Penalties**:
* **个人处罚**：

  * **Civil/Administrative**: Forfeiture of all commission income and substantial fines.
  * **民事/行政**：没收所有佣金收入并处以巨额罚款。

  * **Criminal**: If fraudulent intent (Scienter) is proven, the individual may be charged with wire fraud or securities fraud, leading to long-term imprisonment.
  * **刑事**：如果证明有欺诈意图（故意），该个人可能被指控电信欺诈或证券欺诈，导致长期监禁。

### 9.3.4 **盗取或挪用客户资金 Misappropriation or Embezzlement of Client Funds (Churning & Embezzlement)**

* **Regulation**: Strictly prohibited from moving client funds without authorization, or generating excessive commissions through high-frequency trading (Churning).
* **监管**：严禁未经授权转移客户资金，或通过高频交易（炒单）产生过多佣金。

* **Individual Penalties**:
* **个人处罚**：

  * **Criminal Liability**: Almost certain to involve criminal prosecution, facing **5-20 years** of imprisonment.
  * **刑事责任**：几乎肯定会涉及刑事起诉，面临 **5-20 年**的监禁。

  * **Restitution**: Must return all misappropriated funds and accrued interest.
  * **赔偿**：必须归还所有挪用的资金和应计利息。

### 9.3.5 **以下例题（Practice Questions）**

| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| **2.In U.S. securities law. If an individual violates the regulations regarding Insider Trading & Illegal Information Disclosure, what is the maximum amount of money they can be penalized?**<br />**2. 在美国证券法中。如果个人违反有关内幕交易和非法信息披露的规定，他们最高会被处以多少金额的罚款？**<br />A) $5 million<br />A) 500 万美元<br />B) $50,000<br />B) 5 万美元<br />C) $1 million<br />C) 100 万美元 | **Answer: A**<br />**答案：A**<br />**Explanation:** The DOJ can file criminal charges, leading to a maximum of **20 years** in federal prison and individual criminal fines of up to **$5 million**.<br />**解释：** 司法部可以提起刑事指控，导致最高 **20 年**的联邦监禁和最高 **500 万美元**的个人刑事罚款。 |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
